# EVOLVED_LEAN — Frozen external research report and audit intake

**Analytical label:** `EVOLVED_LEAN`  
**Research state:** frozen external evidence corpus  
**Access date for web sources:** 2026-08-11  
**Repository boundary:** no IMPLEMENTAUDIT repository or other target repository was inspected or analysed.

`EVOLVED_LEAN` is not asserted to be a historical method, Toyota programme, certification or single modern methodology. It is the denominator-backed extraction of engineering properties that remain defensible after tracing TPS, Lean production, Lean Thinking, product-development and software translations, labour and organisational criticism, operations science, domain outcomes and post-disruption resilience work.

## Frozen synthesis

The evidence does **not** support the master rule “eliminate waste, minimise inventory, maximise utilisation, copy Toyota and run kaizen events.” The defensible remainder is closer to a conditional socio-technical control-and-learning architecture:

> Define constrained beneficiary outcomes; expose end-to-end state, queues and abnormalities; control commitments and batch economics only where that improves flow; choose rather than deny variability buffers; build quality and bounded escalation/recovery into work; maintain explicit current baselines that can be challenged and retired; qualify countermeasures with evidence; develop worker and supplier capability without transferring risk coercively; preserve alternatives where uncertainty and irreversibility justify them; and couple speed to reliability, resilience and recovery.

This is a tested synthesis, not an authenticity claim. A property is retained only with its trigger, cheap path, preconditions, failure modes, contrary evidence and anti-ceremony boundary. Harmful deployments are not dismissed merely as “not real Lean.”

## Denominator and freeze rule

The frozen denominator contains **40** final properties:

- **32 crosswalk-worthy positive, evolved, conditional or socio-technical properties** (`EL-001`–`EL-032`);
- **8 superseded, rejected or ceremony-only propositions** (`EL-033`–`EL-040`) retained in the denominator to prevent cherry-picking.

All 14 provisional `EL-HYP-*` candidates from instalment 1 are explicitly mapped to final dispositions. No provisional candidate disappears silently. No complete property remains unexamined; several retain explicit open questions or mixed evidence.

## Unresolved-burden closure

The following table closes or truthfully dispositions every burden carried forward from Research instalment 1. “Closed” here means that the relevant search and adjudication were completed; it does not force certainty where the evidence remains contextual or contested.

| BURDEN | DISPOSITION | FINDING | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Exact TWI→Toyota transmission | CLOSED_WITH_BOUNDED_LINEAGE | Toyota documents TWI's US origin and 1950 Ministry-of-Labour introduction. Participant testimony supports selective uptake: Job Instruction remained important; Job Methods was judged insufficient and replaced/adapted; Job Relations was broadened. This is supervisor-training ancestry, not proof that TWI authored TPS. | [S008], [S009] |
| Exact Deming/statistical-quality influence by property | CLOSED_WITH_SHARED_ANCESTRY | Toyota introduced SQC in 1949, developed control-chart and recurrence-control practice, institutionalised TQC from 1961 and won the Deming Application Prize in 1965. The defensible lineage is to variation visibility, process capability, iterative study and company-wide quality—not to JIT, kanban or jidoka. | [S010], [S011], [S012], [S041] |
| Worker authority versus participation | CLOSED_AS_CONTESTED_SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONDITION | Primary TPS sources describe participation and signalling, but empirical evidence shows participation/autonomy can decline. Final properties split signal authority, local change, team change, cross-functional change and system-owner authority; no blanket empowerment claim survives. | [S006], [S013], [S025], [S026], [S027], [S031] |
| Employment security/trust as precondition | RETAINED_AS_SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT_PROPERTY | Toyota's 1950 conflict and 1962 compact show that stability/trust were institutional, historically contingent issues. Security and fair distribution are strongest where workers are expected to expose labour-saving ideas; they are not universal guarantees of Lean. | [S013], [S014], [S023], [S025], [S031] |
| Supplier development versus supplier risk transfer | CLOSED_BY_SPLIT | Supplier knowledge sharing and capability development are retained separately from multi-tier resilience. Universal synchronisation, concentration and buyer-imposed buffer removal are rejected because disruption and labour/cost can be transferred downstream. | [S038], [S035], [S037], [S039], [S040], [S069] |
| Inventory/capacity/time/information buffer economics | CLOSED_WITH_STRONG_REFINEMENT | Pull is an explicit WIP bound, not a card; variability is absorbed through inventory, capacity or time, supplemented in practice by information, redundancy and scope/recovery options. Inventory minimum is replaced by governed buffer-portfolio economics. | [S032], [S033], [S040] |
| Takt and levelling under high variability | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Classic takt/heijunka remains useful for repetitive measurable demand with controllable mix and setup. High-mix/low-volume conditions need grouping, workload control, dynamic scheduling and explicit residual-variation buffers; no general knowledge-work takt survives. | [S004], [S005], [S034], [S063], [S064] |
| Jidoka/stop authority under false alarms and throughput pressure | CLOSED_WITH_STAGE_MODEL | Final model separates define, detect, signal, triage, contain/stop, restore, diagnose, countermeasure, qualify/restart and recurrence learning. Stop scope depends on consequence, detector quality, response capability and safe authority; the physical cord and unconditional global stop are rejected. | [S001], [S004], [S005], [S031], [S067] |
| Standard-work change authority, expiry and retirement | CLOSED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Historical standardised work supports explicit takt/sequence/standard-WIP baselines. Mature form adds version, purpose, exceptions, change authority, qualification, freshness review, expiry and retirement. The latter are evolved governance safeguards rather than claims about universal historical Toyota practice. | [S004], [S008], [S020], [S026], [S031], [S043], [S044] |
| Causal evidence for kaizen/A3/5-Whys/problem-solving templates | CLOSED_BY_CEREMONY_STRIPPING | Kaizen outcomes depend on leadership, ownership and sustainment; Five Whys can produce shallow arbitrary roots; PDSA is often used without iterative tests, predictions or time-series evidence. Evidence-bound hypotheses and qualified countermeasures survive; formats/counts do not. | [S043], [S044], [S045], [S046], [S047], [S048] |
| Software-flow metric gaming | CLOSED_AS_PERSISTENT_RISK | Software Kanban evidence reports lead-time/coordination benefits but is early and case-heavy. DORA/SPACE-style measures are diagnostic system signals, not value or individual productivity truth; balanced outcomes, definition stability and anti-gaming checks are required. | [S055], [S056], [S058], [S059] |
| Outcome evidence beyond local process improvement | CLOSED_WITH_DOMAIN_PARTITION | Manufacturing meta-analysis supports average performance benefit with heterogeneity. Healthcare reviews find process/flow gains more often than patient, worker or financial gains. Local cycle-time or waste reduction is not accepted as end-outcome proof. | [S060], [S061], [S062] |
| Lean product development/set-based design | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT_BUT_RETAINED | Cross-functional integration and reusable trade-off knowledge are retained. Set-based option preservation is triggered by uncertainty, coupling and costly irreversibility; its comparative empirical support is materially weaker than its conceptual/case literature. | [S049], [S050], [S051], [S052] |
| Lean software/Kanban translation boundaries | CLOSED_WITH_LINEAGE_BOUNDARY | Lean software is a practitioner translation; software Kanban abstracts visualisation and WIP control; Continuous Delivery/DevOps independently add automation, observability, reliability and recovery. No physical board or direct full-TPS ancestry is presumed. | [S053], [S054], [S055], [S056], [S057], [S058] |
| Post-disruption JIT/resilience hybrids | CLOSED_WITH_HYBRID | Neither stockpiling nor JIT guarantees resilience. Mature form segments dependencies and chooses viable stock, capacity, alternate-source, regionalisation, information and recovery options according to criticality, lead time and turbulence. | [S033], [S035], [S036], [S037], [S039], [S040] |

## Evidence and source protocol

- Toyota corporate history and participant writings are used as primary evidence for chronology, stated design and internal doctrine, but not treated as neutral proof of outcomes.
- Later Lean interpretations are labelled as interpretations rather than silently projected backward into historical TPS.
- Labour, health, operations, supply-chain, quality, product-development, software and domain reviews have equal standing when they contradict advocacy.
- Mechanistic, historical, empirical and normative support are distinguished in each property’s `EVIDENCE_STRENGTH`.
- Exact Toyota tools, forms and physical devices are never treated as intrinsically valuable.
- Famous attributions with weak documentary provenance remain narrowed or rejected.
- Citations in the report use source IDs such as `[S032]`; the complete source register below supplies stable URL/DOI, locator, claim, limitation and access date.

## Source register

### S001 — The Birth of Jidoka

- **Author/organisation:** Toyota Motor Corporation
- **Date/version/edition:** Toyota 75-Year History, published 2012
- **Source class:** primary corporate historical archive
- **Exact section/page/locator:** Part 1, ch. 1, sec. 1, item 4
- **Claim supported:** The 1897 Toyoda Power Loom stopped automatically on weft failure, preventing defective continuation and reducing continuous machine watching.
- **Relation to properties:** EL-003, EL-011, EL-012, EL-015
- **Contrary evidence or limit:** Retrospective corporate history; establishes an antecedent, not the complete later TPS doctrine.
- **Evidence label:** `SOURCE_ESTABLISHED`
- **Stable URL / DOI / publisher locator:** https://www.toyota-global.com/company/history_of_toyota/75years/text/taking_on_the_automotive_business/chapter1/section1/item4.html
- **Access date:** 2026-08-11

- **Exact section/page/locator:** deployment pipeline, automated testing, small changes, configuration and release chapters
- **Claim supported:** Continuous Delivery makes small software changes economical by pairing flow with automation and release/recovery engineering.
- **Relation to properties:** EL-005, EL-006, EL-013, EL-026, EL-031
- **Contrary evidence or limit:** Practitioner source; the pipeline can automate bad tests or accelerate low-value work.
- **Evidence label:** `SOURCE_INTERPRETATION`
- **Stable URL / DOI / publisher locator:** https://www.oreilly.com/library/view/continuous-delivery-reliable/9780321670250/

## EVOLVED_LEAN_TIMELINE

This frozen timeline preserves instalment-1 chronology and adds the evidence closures required for the final property extraction. It is not a restart of the genealogy.

| DATE | EVENT | SIGNIFICANCE | LINEAGE | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1897 | Toyoda power loom automatically stops when weft breaks or runs out. | Early abnormality detection, defect prevention and separation of routine machine operation from constant human watching. | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | [S001] |
| 1938 | Kiichiro Toyoda articulates making only what is needed without shortage or excess and seeks flow at the Koromo plant. | Documented JIT intent precedes the later supermarket/kanban implementation. | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | [S002] |
| 1939–1945 | Wartime rationing and supply constraints disrupt exact-time/quantity production. | JIT is historically contingent on supply and information conditions, not a context-free zero-inventory rule. | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | [S002] |
| 1948–1953 | Toyota experiments with pull, visual signals, multi-machine tending, takt/sequence/standard-WIP standardised work and early andon. | TPS components assemble incrementally; no single tool or inventor constitutes the system. | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | [S004], [S005] |
| 1949–1950 | Toyota introduces statistical quality-control study and control charts. | Variation and recurrence control are a shared quality ancestry that develops alongside JIT. | SHARED_QUALITY_ANCESTRY | [S010], [S011] |
| 1950 | TWI reaches Japan through the Ministry of Labour; Toyota adopts supervisor training modules. | Selective transmission is documented: Job Instruction persisted, while Job Methods and Job Relations were adapted or replaced; TWI did not author TPS as a whole. | SHARED_QUALITY_ANCESTRY | [S008], [S009] |
| 1950 | Toyota's severe financial and labour dispute includes proposed job reductions and Kiichiro's resignation. | Employment security and trust cannot be presumed from later respect-for-people doctrine. | HISTORICAL_TPS_SOURCE | [S014] |
| 1950–1951 | Toyota studies Ford operations and bases its suggestion system on Ford's example. | Ford is both a benchmark and a documented source; Toyota selected and transformed rather than simply rejected mass production. | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | [S004], [S019] |
| 1954 | Toyota implements downstream withdrawal/replenishment after an industry article on a Lockheed 'supermarket method'; kanban becomes an instruction signal. | Corrects the personal-supermarket-visit myth and distinguishes pull property from card implementation. | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | [S003] |
| 1955 onward | Shigeo Shingo teaches production-analysis P-Course material to Toyota personnel. | Material training/industrial-engineering contribution; broad claims that Shingo invented JIT, jidoka, kanban or TPS remain unsupported. | SHARED_QUALITY_ANCESTRY | [S009] |
| 1961–1965 | Toyota formalises company-wide TQC and receives the Deming Application Prize in 1965. | Quality-system and participation infrastructure is strengthened; this is not evidence that Deming originated JIT or jidoka. | SHARED_QUALITY_ANCESTRY | [S010], [S011], [S012] |
| 1962 | Toyota and its union sign a declaration linking mutual trust, productivity, working conditions and employment stability. | A labour compact supported the production system but remained an institutional commitment whose practice must be tested empirically. | SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT | [S013], [S014] |
| 1963–1966 | Kanban expands across plants and suppliers; levelling, maintenance, supplier quality and visible supervisory response develop. | Pull becomes a networked capability dependent on reliability, setup reduction, information and supplier conditions. | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | [S004], [S005], [S069] |
| 1971–1975 | Fixed-position stopping, sub-ten-minute setup, broader standardised work and in-process quality measures are recorded. | Small lots and stop authority were engineered with response windows, buffers and setup economics—not applied as absolutes. | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | [S004], [S005] |
| 1977 | Sugimori, Kusunoki, Cho and Uchikawa publish an English TPS account. | Contemporary primary source identifies JIT/minimum stock and a respect-for-human participation system; doctrine is not outcome proof. | HISTORICAL_TPS_SOURCE | [S006] |
| 1978 Japanese / 1988 English | Ohno publishes Toyota Production System: Beyond Large-Scale Production. | Central participant codification, retrospective and author-centred rather than complete institutional history. | HISTORICAL_TPS_SOURCE | [S007] |
| 1988 | John Krafcik publishes 'Triumph of the Lean Production System.' | 'Lean' emerges as an external comparative analytical category, not Toyota's original name for TPS. | LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION | [S016] |
| 1990 | The Machine That Changed the World popularises the IMVP craft/mass/Lean comparison. | Lean becomes a global transformation narrative with stronger transferability claims than the Toyota-specific historical record warrants. | LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION | [S017], [S024] |
| 1991–1999 | Automotive product-development research identifies integration, heavyweight leadership and set-based concurrent engineering. | A distinct product-development lineage preserves alternatives under uncertainty rather than eliminating apparent redundancy immediately. | LEAN_PRODUCT_DEVELOPMENT_LINEAGE | [S049], [S050], [S051], [S052] |
| 1992–1999 | Economic, labour and health critiques challenge universal superiority and empowerment claims. | Actual Lean effects are contested; tool-copying is not the only source of harm. | REACTION_TO_LEAN | [S024], [S025] |
| 1996 | Lean Thinking generalises value, value stream, flow, pull and perfection beyond automotive manufacturing. | Conceptual portability increases while Toyota-specific labour, maintenance, supplier and governance institutions recede. | LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION | [S018] |
| 1999–2009 | Learning-system, socio-technical, contingency and construct-definition interpretations mature. | Lean shifts from visible tools toward hypotheses, connections, culture and bundles, while definitional ambiguity remains. | LATER_LEAN_INTERPRETATION | [S020], [S021], [S022], [S023] |
| 2001 | Toyota formalises Continuous Improvement and Respect for People in Toyota Way 2001. | Later doctrine strengthens social constraints but does not retroactively establish worker experience. | LATER_TPS_CODIFICATION | [S015] |
| 2003–2010 | Lean software, software Kanban and Continuous Delivery translate or converge on WIP, small-batch feedback and automation. | Physical cards and factory routing are not required; quality, architecture, observability and recovery become decisive software conditions. | SOFTWARE_TRANSLATION / HYBRID | [S053], [S054], [S055], [S057] |
| 2004 | Hopp and Spearman define tactical pull by an explicit WIP limit and Lean by the cost of buffering variability. | Operations science replaces inventory ideology with explicit inventory/capacity/time buffer economics. | CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING | [S032] |
| 2003–2022 | Longitudinal and review evidence documents both positive participatory cases and autonomy loss, work intensification and stress. | Worker authority, support, security and work design are causal conditions, not automatic consequences of Lean. | REACTION_TO_LEAN | [S026], [S027], [S028], [S029], [S031], [S070] |
| 2011–2023 | Inventory-performance and disruption research qualify extreme leanness; Toyota maps deeper tiers and later literature advocates JIT/JIC segmentation. | Strategic buffers, multi-tier visibility and recovery capability become legitimate evolved properties. | REACTION_TO_LEAN / HYBRID | [S033], [S035], [S036], [S037], [S039], [S040] |
| 2014–2017 | PDSA, Five Whys and root-cause-analysis research exposes low-fidelity iteration and shallow single-cause narratives. | Prediction-bearing tests and qualified countermeasures survive; exact templates and counts do not. | SHARED_QUALITY_ANCESTRY / REACTION_TO_LEAN | [S045], [S046], [S047], [S048] |
| 2016–2020 | Healthcare reviews report frequent flow gains but weak or mixed patient, worker and financial outcomes. | Local process improvement cannot stand in for system or beneficiary outcome evidence. | DOMAIN_TRANSLATION | [S060], [S061] |
| 2018–2026 | Modern software delivery research combines flow with stability, observability, reliability, recovery and multidimensional productivity safeguards. | The mature software hybrid rejects speed-only or single-metric Lean translation. | CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING / HYBRID | [S058], [S059] |

## EVOLVED_LEAN_GENEALOGY

| NODE | FROM | TO | CLASSIFICATION | BOUNDARY | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Jidoka antecedent | Toyoda textile loom defect-stop | Automotive detection, signal, bounded stop and human exception response | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | The physical loom mechanism is not a universal implementation; detection without response or learning is incomplete. | [S001], [S004], [S067] |
| JIT intent | Kiichiro's needed quantity/time and flow | Ohno-era downstream withdrawal, kanban and levelling | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | JIT means neither zero inventory nor make-to-order everywhere. | [S002], [S003], [S005], [S006] |
| Ford influence | Flow, industrial scale and suggestion-system observation | Selective adaptation for low-volume, mixed production | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | Toyota both learned from and departed from Ford; a simple opposition is false. | [S004], [S019] |
| Supermarket/replenishment | Lockheed article describing a supermarket method | Downstream withdrawal and upstream replenishment | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | The famous personal US grocery-store visit is not established by Toyota's documentary account. | [S003] |
| TWI supervisor training | US wartime JI/JM/JR, introduced to Japan by Ministry of Labour | Toyota supervisor instruction, relations and improvement curricula | SHARED_QUALITY_ANCESTRY | Selective absorption; no evidence that TWI generated the full TPS architecture. | [S008], [S009] |
| SQC/TQC/Deming | Statistical control and company-wide quality traditions | Variation visibility, recurrence control, quality participation and iterative learning | SHARED_QUALITY_ANCESTRY | Supports quality/problem-solving properties, not provenance claims for JIT, kanban or jidoka. | [S010], [S011], [S012], [S041] |
| Toyota labour institutions | 1950 conflict and 1962 labour-management compact | Later mutual-trust, stability and respect doctrine | SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT | Doctrine and employment compact are not proof of autonomy or benign workload in every deployment. | [S013], [S014], [S015], [S025], [S026], [S028], [S029] |
| Supplier network | Supplier associations, quality support and kanban extension | Knowledge sharing and coordinated replenishment | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | Capability development must be separated from cost/risk transfer, concentration and opaque lower tiers. | [S038], [S069], [S035], [S037], [S040] |
| Lean production | IMVP plant comparisons and Krafcik's naming | External comparative production model | LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION | Not identical to Toyota's historical system and institutions. | [S016], [S017], [S019], [S024] |
| Lean Thinking | Lean production | Value/value-stream/flow/pull/perfection enterprise abstraction | LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION | Greater portability creates under-specification of buffers, labour, reliability and causal evidence. | [S018], [S022], [S023] |
| Learning-system interpretation | Observed Toyota specification and improvement routines | Explicit hypotheses, abnormality exposure and coached experimentation | LATER_LEAN_INTERPRETATION | Useful model, but not proof that historical or deployed Lean always operated scientifically. | [S020], [S047], [S048] |
| Lean product development | Automotive product-development comparisons | Cross-functional integration, trade-off knowledge and set-based convergence | LEAN_PRODUCT_DEVELOPMENT_LINEAGE | Not factory Lean applied verbatim; evidence is concentrated in engineering design contexts. | [S049], [S050], [S051], [S052] |
| Lean software | Selected Lean principles | Software learning, quality, flow and waste translation | SOFTWARE_TRANSLATION | Code, queues and discovery are not physical inventory; original evidence was practitioner-led. | [S053] |
| Software Kanban | Pull/WIP ideas plus evolutionary change | Workflow visualisation and WIP constraints in knowledge work | SOFTWARE_TRANSLATION / HYBRID | Board/card is optional; empirical literature was initially small, case-heavy and context-sensitive. | [S054], [S055], [S056] |
| Continuous Delivery / DevOps | Software engineering automation, reliability and operations plus flow convergence | Small changes, automated qualification, observability and recovery | CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING / HYBRID | Do not claim all DevOps capabilities as direct TPS descendants; delivery metrics are gameable proxies. | [S057], [S058], [S059] |
| Post-disruption resilience | JIT criticism and supply-chain disruption evidence | Segmented JIT/JIC, multi-tier visibility, strategic inventory/capacity/time buffers | REACTION_TO_LEAN / HYBRID | Neither inventory nor JIT alone guarantees resilience. | [S033], [S035], [S036], [S037], [S039], [S040] |

### Provisional-to-final hypothesis crosswalk

| PROVISIONAL_ID | FINAL_DISPOSITION | NOTE |
| --- | --- | --- |
| EL-HYP-01 | EL-011; EL-012 | Split abnormality visibility from stop/containment/escalation/recovery authority. |
| EL-HYP-02 | EL-013 | Retained as quality at source and defect containment. |
| EL-HYP-03 | EL-017; EL-040 | Retained only as versioned current baseline; immutable standardisation rejected. |
| EL-HYP-04 | EL-018 | Retained with traceable system evidence; direct observation alone is insufficient. |
| EL-HYP-05 | EL-002; EL-003 | Split end-to-end flow visibility from local work-state legibility. |
| EL-HYP-06 | EL-004; EL-005; EL-036 | Conditional WIP and batch economics retained; smallest-batch-everywhere rejected. |
| EL-HYP-07 | EL-006 | Strongly retained as setup/release/feedback latency reduction. |
| EL-HYP-08 | EL-008; EL-034; EL-035 | Governed buffer portfolio retained; universal inventory and slack minimisation rejected. |
| EL-HYP-09 | EL-021; EL-022; EL-023 | Expanded into capability, safe authority and employment/trust conditions. |
| EL-HYP-10 | EL-019; EL-020; EL-038 | Evidence-bound qualification and sustainment retained; improvement activity counts rejected. |
| EL-HYP-11 | EL-009; EL-025; EL-035 | Whole-system flow and proxy governance retained; maximum utilisation rejected. |
| EL-HYP-12 | EL-027; EL-028; EL-039 | Capability and dependency visibility retained; universal synchronisation/concentration rejected. |
| EL-HYP-13 | EL-029 | Context-dependent set-based option preservation. |
| EL-HYP-14 | EL-024 | Strongly retained as management responsibility for structural conditions and response. |

## TPS_VS_LEAN_VS_TOOLKIT_CARICATURE

| OBJECT | DEFINITION | VALID_CORE | OMISSION_OR_RISK | CLASS | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Historical TPS | Toyota-specific production, quality, maintenance, training, labour-management and supplier system assembled over decades. | Interacting JIT, jidoka, capability, quality, response and institutional arrangements. | Toyota histories are retrospective; practice varied over periods and sites. | HISTORICAL_TPS_SOURCE | [S001], [S002], [S003], [S004], [S005], [S006], [S014] |
| Lean production | External IMVP comparative category for a production configuration associated with high productivity, quality and variety. | Comparative whole-plant configuration rather than one tool. | Universal superiority and craft/mass contrasts were disputed; plant outcomes can omit labour and network effects. | LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION | [S016], [S017], [S024] |
| Lean Thinking | Portable enterprise principles of value, value stream, flow, pull and perfection. | Moves attention to end-to-end customer pathways and continuous improvement. | Abstracts away much Toyota-specific reliability, buffers, labour compact and supplier governance. | LATER_LEAN_INTERPRETATION | [S018], [S022], [S023] |
| Toolkit Lean | Installation of 5S, kanban, A3, VSM, visual boards, kaizen events and standard forms. | Some tools cheaply instantiate useful properties in the right context. | Artefact presence is confused with mechanism, capability or outcome. | TOOLKIT_CARICATURE | [S020], [S022], [S030], [S066] |
| Lean and mean | Cost/headcount reduction, tighter pace and buffer removal under Lean terminology. | None as a general Lean property; some cost pressure has historical roots. | Can destroy trust, safety, learning, resilience and worker well-being while transferring cost. | REAL_DEPLOYED_PRACTICE | [S014], [S025], [S026], [S028], [S029], [S070] |
| Learning-system Lean | Later interpretation centred on explicit baselines, abnormalities, hypotheses and coached experiments. | Preserves evidence-bound learning independent of Toyota tools. | Can romanticise work and power relations or label low-fidelity templates 'science'. | LATER_LEAN_INTERPRETATION | [S020], [S047], [S048] |
| Socio-technical Lean | Integrated technical and social bundle whose effects depend on work design, voice, skills, trust and management response. | Treats people conditions as causal rather than decorative. | No universally agreed bundle or automatic benefit; effects remain heterogeneous. | SOURCE_INTERPRETATION / CONTESTED | [S021], [S027], [S028], [S030], [S031] |
| EVOLVED_LEAN | Analytical extraction of surviving engineering properties, including rejected forms and explicit contexts. | Mechanism-, trigger-, evidence- and failure-mode-level adjudication with cheap paths and anti-ceremony boundaries. | Not a historical method or brand; it must not become another toolkit or authenticity claim. | ANALYTICAL_LABEL | [S019], [S022], [S032] |

## EVOLVED_LEAN_PROPERTY_LEDGER

### Frozen population overview

The denominator includes the negative and ceremony-only propositions because a future crosswalk must be able to detect harmful or redundant Lean imports, not merely match attractive survivors.

| PROPERTY_ID | PROPERTY_NAME | CURRENT_STATUS | EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | LINEAGE | AUTHORITY |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EL-001 | Outcome and value definition with named beneficiary, constraints and time horizon | STRONGLY_RETAINED | MODERATE_CONVERGENT | LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION | SYSTEM_OWNER |
| EL-002 | End-to-end system boundary and flow/queue/handoff visibility | STRONGLY_RETAINED | HIGH_CONVERGENT | LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION | CROSS_FUNCTIONAL |
| EL-003 | Workplace and work-state legibility | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | MODERATE_EMPIRICAL | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | TEAM |
| EL-004 | Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL | DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE | CROSS_FUNCTIONAL |

### EL-001 — Outcome and value definition with named beneficiary, constraints and time horizon

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-001
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Outcome and value definition with named beneficiary, constraints and time horizon
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Toyota's customer-first quality doctrine; Lean Thinking's value principle; later service/product critiques.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Define value from the customer and orient production toward it.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Organisations can efficiently produce outputs that do not improve the beneficiary's outcome or that externalise cost.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Efficient delivery of low-value, harmful or locally beneficial work.
- **MECHANISM:** Name the beneficiary and desired outcome; state safety, labour, resilience and externality constraints; choose a time horizon; test whether work changes the outcome.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Multiple plausible outputs compete, resources are material, or local efficiency could displace customer/system value.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For trivial, reversible work, record a one-sentence purpose and acceptance condition rather than create a value-stream artefact.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Legitimate beneficiary representation; outcome observability; explicit non-negotiable constraints; authority to stop low-value work.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Better selection, fewer abandoned outputs, reduced proxy optimisation and clearer trade-offs.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Customer rhetoric masks managerial cost goals; one stakeholder's value harms another; short-horizon metrics; unmeasurable outcomes; retrospective rationalisation.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Lean literature specifies waste more richly than value; customer language can ignore workers, suppliers, public risk and long-term resilience.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Moved from production value and waste categories toward multi-stakeholder outcome framing, product discovery and system constraints.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use an explicit value hypothesis with beneficiary, outcome, constraints, evidence threshold, review date and abandonment rule.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** No Lean vocabulary, value workshop or value-stream map is required; the property is a decision-relevant outcome model.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** STRONGLY_RETAINED
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_CONVERGENT
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S010], [S011], [S018]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S024], [S060], [S065]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S062], [S060], [S061]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should value be aggregated when customers, workers, suppliers and public safety conflict?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** SYSTEM_OWNER
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-009
  - EL-023
  - EL-008
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the target system name the beneficiary, desired outcome, constraints and time horizon rather than equating output or waste removal with value?
  - Can it stop or retire work when the value hypothesis fails?
  - Are labour, safety, resilience and external costs prevented from disappearing behind a customer-value claim?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-002 — End-to-end system boundary and flow/queue/handoff visibility

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-002
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** End-to-end system boundary and flow/queue/handoff visibility
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** TPS material/information flow; Lean value-stream generalisation; later service/software translations.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** See the whole path from demand through production and delivery rather than optimise isolated machines or departments.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Local gains create downstream queues, rework, handoff loss or delayed customer outcomes.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Local optimisation and invisible waiting across organisational boundaries.
- **MECHANISM:** Define the relevant path and work classes; observe arrivals, queues, processing, rework, handoffs, dependencies and outcome latency; assign end-to-end ownership.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Repeated handoffs, unexplained elapsed time, departmental optimisation or customer delay dominate processing time.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For a short single-owner path, a simple trace or sampled timeline is enough; no wall-sized map.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable item identity; traceability across boundaries; truthful timestamps/states; an owner able to resolve cross-boundary causes.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Reduced total lead time, fewer handoff failures, better bottleneck and queue decisions.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Map theatre; stale diagrams; excessive instrumentation; forced linear path for exploratory work; omission of externalities; local teams unable to change system constraints.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Value-stream maps are frequently local snapshots and can mistake visible factory-like sequence for the real distributed causal system.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Value-stream mapping was generalised beyond factories; modern software combines traces and delivery telemetry, while discovery work may require networks rather than linear streams.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Maintain the cheapest decision-useful end-to-end model, including feedback/rework loops, queues, dependencies and outcome—not just touch time.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A value-stream map is one implementation. Event logs, service blueprints, dependency graphs or sampled traces may be stronger.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** STRONGLY_RETAINED
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S003], [S005], [S018]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S060], [S065]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S020], [S061], [S058]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should boundaries be chosen in multi-product, platform or ecosystem settings without creating an unmanageable total map?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** LEAN_PRODUCTION_GENERALISATION
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** CROSS_FUNCTIONAL
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** End-to-end latency, queue and handoff profile; assumes valid item identity and boundary. Over-instrumentation and forced linearisation are failure modes.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-018
  - EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system expose end-to-end waiting, queues, handoffs, rework and outcome latency rather than only local activity?
  - Is the map or trace current enough to change a decision?
  - Does it preserve a cheap path where the work is already short, observable and single-owner?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-003 — Workplace and work-state legibility

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-003
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Workplace and work-state legibility
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** TPS visual control/5S/andon; later service and software visualisation.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Arrange physical work so expected state, ownership, missing items and abnormalities are easy to discern.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Search, ambiguity and hidden state delay action or conceal abnormal work.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Undetected abnormality, lost work, excessive search and ambiguous ownership.
- **MECHANISM:** Expose state and limits at the point of work using labels, layout, telemetry, state models or visual cues proportional to decision need.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Work is repeatedly lost, searched for, misrouted, duplicated or left in an ambiguous state.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** When state is already obvious and low-risk, use naming conventions or a small status field; do not create ritual boards or audit scoring.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Shared state definitions; maintenance ownership; accessibility; protection against surveillance and stale displays.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Faster recognition, lower search/coordination cost, earlier abnormality detection.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** 5S audit theatre; cosmetic tidiness; information overload; stale boards; worker surveillance; local legibility hiding end-to-end state.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Evidence for exact 5S bundles is small and heterogeneous; legibility can become discipline and scoring rather than aid.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Expanded from physical workplace order to digital work-state and observability; mature form separates human legibility from metric control.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Make the minimum relevant normal/abnormal state visible to people who can act, with freshness, accessibility and ownership controls.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** 5S labels, shadow boards and kanban columns are optional; cleanliness scores are not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_EMPIRICAL
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S001], [S004], [S005]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S025], [S026], [S066]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S066], [S055], [S061]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How much legibility improves outcomes independently of accompanying management attention remains weakly identified.

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** TEAM
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-023
  - EL-025
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Can responsible people tell the current and abnormal state without reconstructing it manually?
  - Are displays fresh, action-linked and non-punitive rather than ceremonial surveillance?
  - Would additional visual management solve a real ambiguity or merely add another board?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-004 — Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-004
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Toyota supermarket/kanban; operations-science formalisation; software Kanban translation.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Downstream withdrawal and explicit limits prevent premature production and excess work in process.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Unbounded release creates congestion, long feedback, priority churn and hidden defects.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Queue growth, overproduction, premature commitment and excessive context switching.
- **MECHANISM:** Set an explicit bound or release rule for active commitments; admit new work when capacity becomes available; define exceptions and service classes.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Arrival pressure exceeds completion capacity, active work accumulates, or long WIP correlates with delay/quality loss.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For low-volume work with naturally bounded commitments, use a simple finish-before-start norm or periodic capacity check.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Observable WIP boundary; stable item definitions; capacity/replenishment capability; priority and exception policy; no hidden pre-entry queue.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Shorter and more predictable lead time, clearer constraints, faster defect feedback.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Starvation; hidden backlog before entry; arbitrary limits; gaming item size/status; blocking critical long-lead work; apparent cycle-time gains through exclusion.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** A pull signal is not trustworthy demand or capacity; board-level limits can displace queues and invite metric gaming.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Pull was clarified as WIP limitation rather than cards or make-to-order; software translation retained limits but evidence shows optimal limits are contextual.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use empirical limits by work class, monitor blocked/aged work and total demand, and adapt limits with capacity, variability and service risk.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Kanban cards/boards are not required. A database rule, token, CONWIP, admission policy or finite-capacity scheduler can implement it.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S003], [S005], [S006]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S032], [S033], [S039]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S032], [S034], [S055], [S056]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** What causal evidence is sufficient to distinguish a useful limit from demand suppression or work reclassification?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** CROSS_FUNCTIONAL
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: congestion and feedback reduction. Assumes a valid boundary and enough response capacity. Over-application causes starvation or displaced queues. Pair with EL-008 and EL-010.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-007
  - EL-008
  - EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the target system explicitly limit active commitments or merely display them?
  - Are hidden upstream queues, work-item splitting and exception classes visible?
  - Does the limit trigger only where congestion is a demonstrated failure mode?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-005 — Batch-size economics and small reversible increments

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-005
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Batch-size economics and small reversible increments
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** TPS setup reduction and small lots; Lean product development and Continuous Delivery translation.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Reduce lot size to shorten feedback and lower inventory once setup costs permit.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Large batches delay information, amplify rework and make failures expensive.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Late defect discovery, large release risk, long queue time and irreversible commitment.
- **MECHANISM:** Model batch benefits against setup, validation, coordination and transport costs; reduce setup first; choose a reversible economic batch.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Feedback delay or failure blast radius is material and switching/release cost can be reduced.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** If changes are already tiny or validation has a fixed high cost, keep the economic batch and improve feedback within it.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurable setup/release costs; modular work; validation capability; rollback/recovery; ability to integrate increments.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Faster learning, smaller failures, lower inventory and release risk.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** One-piece-flow dogma; fragmentation overhead; excessive coordination; local micro-batches accumulating into system batches; unsafe partial delivery.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Smallness is not value; a minimum batch can be uneconomic, and knowledge work may require coherent experimental units.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Generalised from physical lots to information, decisions and software changes; product development added option preservation rather than indiscriminate decomposition.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Select batch size from total economics and reversibility, not a universal minimum; make delayed feedback and blast radius explicit.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** One-piece flow, daily releases or story slicing are implementations, not the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S004], [S007]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S032], [S036]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S057], [S061], [S062]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should economic batch size be estimated where quality evidence is sparse or validation cost is nonlinear?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** HYBRID
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** TEAM
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: shorter feedback and smaller failure scope. Assumes manageable setup/validation. Overuse creates fragmentation and coordination waste.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-006
  - EL-029
  - EL-036
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system choose batch size from feedback, setup, validation and failure economics?
  - Can it use a cheap path when smaller batches would add ceremony or integration cost?
  - Are locally small batches still bundled into a large end-to-end release?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-006 — Setup, changeover, release and feedback latency reduction

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-006
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Setup, changeover, release and feedback latency reduction
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Toyota setup-time reduction and industrial engineering; later software deployment automation.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Lower die-change and process-switching time so mixed production and small lots become economical.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** High transition cost forces large batches, inflexibility and infrequent feedback.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Long feedback loops, large economic batches, downtime and costly recovery.
- **MECHANISM:** Separate necessary from avoidable setup; externalise safe preparation; simplify interfaces; automate repeatable checks; measure total transition and recovery.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Setup, integration, release or environment preparation materially determines batch size, delay or downtime.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** If setup is rare and immaterial, document the path and avoid an optimisation programme.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable interfaces; safe automation; competent maintainers; configuration control; rollback and qualification.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Greater flexibility, smaller economic batches, higher availability and faster learning.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Unsafe shortcuts; preparation shifted to another team; brittle automation; ergonomic or quality risk; faster release of low-value work.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Local setup savings often omit preparation, validation and recovery transferred elsewhere.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Extended from physical die changes to software pipelines, environment provisioning, integration and decision-feedback latency.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Reduce total safe transition cost, including preparation, validation, recovery and burden transferred across boundaries.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** SMED workshops, coloured steps and checklists are optional; lower total transition cost is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** STRONGLY_RETAINED
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S004], [S007]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S024], [S025]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S032], [S057]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** Which transition costs are shifted to suppliers, operations or workers and therefore missed by local measures?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** TEAM
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: enables smaller batches and flexible capacity. Assumes quality and safety are preserved. Overuse hides transferred work or brittle automation.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-013
  - EL-023
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is batch size large because setup, release or feedback is expensive, and has that cost been attacked directly?
  - Does the metric include validation, recovery and transferred work?
  - Would automation lower total safe transition cost or merely automate a fragile process?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-007 — Demand-capacity pacing and production levelling

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-007
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Demand-capacity pacing and production levelling
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Kiichiro/Ohno JIT, takt and heijunka in repetitive automotive production.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Use demand pace and mix levelling to coordinate repetitive production and reduce unevenness.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Uncoordinated release creates surges, starvation, overtime and supplier amplification.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Demand/capacity mismatch, violent mix changes, uneven workloads and hidden overburden.
- **MECHANISM:** Estimate sustainable pace by work class; level the predictable component; explicitly buffer residual variation; replan when assumptions break.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Repeatable work, measurable aggregate demand, flexible capacity and feasible mix sequencing.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For unique, exploratory or very low-volume work, use finite-capacity planning, rolling forecasts and explicit priority rather than takt ceremony.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Demand information; cross-trained/flexible resources; setup capability; exception policy; workload and ergonomic safeguards; buffers.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Smoother flow, less oscillation, clearer overload and more stable supplier/capacity planning.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** False precision; backlog or overtime used to hide variation; forced pace and work intensification; brittle high-mix schedules; supplier burden transfer.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Takt can become a coercive pace standard; conventional levelling has weak applicability in high-mix/low-volume or discovery work.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Later research narrows conventional levelling to stable/repetitive contexts and develops HMLV adaptations; resilience hybrids preserve reserves.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Level only forecastable aggregate demand, maintain capacity/time buffers and protect workload limits; treat takt as diagnostic, not a metronome.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Takt boards, heijunka boxes and fixed pitch are manufacturing implementations, not general requirements.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_CONTEXTUAL
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S002], [S004], [S005]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S025], [S026], [S064]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S034], [S063], [S064]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** Robust rules for levelling highly variable knowledge work remain unresolved; evidence is dominated by manufacturing cases.

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** SYSTEM_OWNER
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: reduces oscillation and overload. Assumes repeatability and demand aggregation. Overuse converts variability into backlog, overtime or supplier stress.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-008
  - EL-023
  - EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is the work repetitive and demand sufficiently observable for a pace or levelling rule to be meaningful?
  - Where does residual variation go—capacity reserve, inventory, delay, overtime or suppliers?
  - Does the mechanism reveal overload or impose an unsafe pace?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-008 — Explicit buffer portfolio and strategic slack

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-008
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Explicit buffer portfolio and strategic slack
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Operations-science refinement of JIT; Toyota capacity practices; post-disruption resilience research.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** JIT sought minimum necessary stock while Toyota also used capacity and time arrangements to absorb variability.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Removing one buffer silently transfers variability and disruption cost to another part of the system.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Fragility, starvation, burnout, missed service, recovery delay and hidden risk transfer.
- **MECHANISM:** Model variability, lead times, failure consequence and recovery; assign each inventory, capacity, time, information or redundancy buffer a purpose, owner, activation trigger, size logic and review date.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Variability or disruption cannot be eliminated economically and service or failure consequences are material.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For low-risk, rapidly recoverable work, keep only a small contingency and explicit recovery path.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Demand/failure data; risk appetite; cross-boundary cost visibility; authority to hold reserve; anti-hoarding review.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Lower expected total cost, better recovery, less emergency expediting and reduced human overburden.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Slack becomes unmanaged delay; reserves are raided for utilisation; inventory obsolescence; redundancy hides defects; false quantitative precision.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Classic Lean rhetoric often treats inventory and slack as waste, while disruption and inventory-performance evidence shows non-monotonic returns.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** JIT absolutism was replaced by minimal total buffering cost and segment-specific JIT/JIC/resilience designs.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Optimise a portfolio of justified buffers; distinguish protective reserve from accidental queue and continuously reassess assumptions.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Safety stock, spare capacity, backup suppliers or time contingencies are implementations. No universal buffer is mandatory.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** STRONGLY_RETAINED
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S002], [S006]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S033], [S039]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S032], [S034], [S040]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should correlated and deep-tier risks be priced when probabilities are poorly known?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** REACTION_TO_LEAN
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** SYSTEM_OWNER
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Core resilience property. Value: absorbs unavoidable variability. Overuse creates cost/latency; underuse creates fragility.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-004
  - EL-007
  - EL-009
  - EL-034
  - EL-035
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system explicitly choose among inventory, capacity, time, information and redundancy buffers?
  - Can it distinguish strategic slack from accidental queueing?
  - Would proposed WIP or inventory reduction merely transfer risk or workload elsewhere?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-009 — Flow and queue-aware capacity over maximum local utilisation

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-009
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Flow and queue-aware capacity over maximum local utilisation
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** TPS attack on overproduction and interprocess queues; queueing/operations-science refinement.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Prevent one process from producing work the next process cannot absorb.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** High utilisation under variability creates nonlinear queues and blocks the system constraint.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Congestion, priority churn, long lead time and local output that cannot be absorbed downstream.
- **MECHANISM:** Measure queue age/size and end-to-end throughput; reserve response capacity; subordinate nonconstraints; stop releasing work that worsens congestion.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Queues grow despite high activity or local productivity gains fail to improve total outcome.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For low variability and ample capacity, simple utilisation monitoring plus exception handling is enough.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Valid system boundary; capacity/arrival data; authority across silos; protected slack; outcome measures.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Lower congestion, shorter lead time, better service and less emergency work.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Underutilisation without purpose; politically contested spare capacity; local teams penalised for system choices; bottleneck migration.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Lean deployments may still pursue labour or machine utilisation and headcount reduction despite flow doctrine.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Anti-overproduction was strengthened by queueing theory and later flow metrics, while resilience research legitimised spare capacity.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Optimise end-to-end service and total buffering cost; treat utilisation as a constraint or signal, not a target to maximise.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Cell layouts or line-balancing charts are not required; queue-aware admission and capacity rules are alternatives.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** STRONGLY_RETAINED
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S003], [S005]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S024], [S025]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S032], [S056], [S062]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should public or safety-critical systems value reserve capacity whose benefit appears only during peaks?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** SYSTEM_OWNER
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: avoids congestion collapse. Assumes a meaningful service boundary. Over-application leaves costly idle capacity; under-application creates nonlinear queues.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-008
  - EL-035
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the target system optimise end-to-end throughput and latency or reward local busy time and output?
  - Is reserve capacity protected where variability makes high utilisation dangerous?
  - Are queue costs and the location of the constraint visible?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-010 — Demand and replenishment information integrity

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-010
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Demand and replenishment information integrity
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Toyota downstream withdrawal/kanban and supplier coordination; later digital flow systems.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Use downstream consumption to signal upstream replenishment.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Pull based on stale, distorted or gameable signals amplifies shortages and excess.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** False demand, bullwhip, duplicate work, priority gaming and long-lead omissions.
- **MECHANISM:** Define signal source, semantics, freshness, authority, forecast complement and exception path; reconcile consumption with long-lead and capacity data.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Replenishment or release decisions rely on demand signals across time or organisational boundaries.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For direct low-volume requests, use explicit confirmation and a simple backlog.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Trusted item identity; data quality; latency bounds; incentives that do not reward signal manipulation; long-lead visibility.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** More accurate release, fewer shortages/excesses and better capacity planning.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Demand is endogenous or manipulated; urgent work bypasses signals; forecasts ignored; local pull cannot prepare long-lead components.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** A visible pull signal does not establish trustworthy demand or adequate replenishment capability.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Physical kanban became digital replenishment and admission policies; mature form combines actual consumption with forecast, risk and capacity.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** A governed demand-information contract, not blind reaction to the latest request.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Physical cards, supermarkets and board movements are optional implementations.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S002], [S003], [S005]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S039], [S040]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S032], [S034], [S035]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should pull incorporate sparse catastrophic demand and strategic long-lead preparation?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** CROSS_FUNCTIONAL
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: truthful release and replenishment. Assumes timely signals and response capability. Failure creates starvation, oscillation or false urgency.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-007
  - EL-008
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is the demand or pull signal timely, semantically clear and resistant to gaming?
  - Does the system complement consumption with long-lead, capacity and risk information?
  - Can urgent exceptions be distinguished from habitual bypass?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-011 — Abnormality detection and signalling

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-011
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Abnormality detection and signalling
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Toyoda loom stop antecedent; Toyota andon and visual control.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Detect an abnormal condition at the source and make it visible to the responsible responder.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Defects, unsafe states and process failures can continue silently until downstream discovery.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Silent propagation, delayed response and normalisation of deviation.
- **MECHANISM:** Define detectable abnormal states and confidence; instrument or enable human recognition; signal with severity, location, owner and time.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Continued operation after an abnormality materially increases harm, rework, delay or uncertainty.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For rare, low-consequence events, use a manual report with clear ownership rather than continuous instrumentation.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Known normal envelope; usable detectors; low-friction human signalling; response ownership; alarm review.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Earlier containment and shorter time to response.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** False alarms, missed detections, alarm fatigue, ambiguous ownership, suppressed signals and throughput pressure.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Detection without response creates theatre; automated detection can encode an incomplete model and workers may be punished for signalling.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Extended from mechanical stops and lights to telemetry, alerts and operator calls; mature form manages precision, severity and responder load.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Detect and signal decision-relevant abnormalities with calibrated severity, accountable response and feedback on false positives/negatives.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** An andon cord or light is optional; alerts, checks, guardrails or human escalation can implement the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** STRONGLY_RETAINED
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S001], [S004], [S005], [S067]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S025], [S029]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S031], [S067]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should detection thresholds be tuned when misses are catastrophic but false alarms erode response capacity?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** WORKER_LOCAL
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-012
  - EL-023
  - EL-025
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system expose material abnormalities early enough to change the outcome?
  - Are detection accuracy, alarm burden and ownership monitored?
  - Can a person signal safely when automated detection is absent or wrong?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-012 — Bounded stop, containment, escalation and recovery authority

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-012
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Bounded stop, containment, escalation and recovery authority
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Jidoka, fixed-position stopping and andon response in Toyota production.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Stop or hold work when an abnormality cannot be resolved within the local cycle.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** A recognised defect or unsafe condition continues because throughput pressure overrides containment.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Defect propagation, unsafe continuation and unmanaged emergency escalation.
- **MECHANISM:** Define severity-based authority to hold, stop, isolate or degrade service; summon support; specify restart/recovery criteria and appeal.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Expected propagation harm exceeds interruption and recovery cost, or safety/quality boundaries are breached.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For low-consequence reversible anomalies, flag and route to a bounded follow-up queue without stopping the whole system.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Psychological/employment safety; available responders; isolation capability; restart authority; false-stop review; recovery path.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Smaller blast radius, faster expert response, safer operation and preserved evidence.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** False stops, alarm fatigue, punitive culture, ignored calls, local stop causing larger system harm, rapid reset without diagnosis.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** A cord does not confer real authority; line pressure can suppress use, and indiscriminate stopping is costly.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** From line stop to tiered containment, circuit breakers, feature flags, quarantine and controlled degradation.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Severity-calibrated, non-punitive stop/containment authority with response time, restart criteria, evidence preservation and post-event learning.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A physical cord, red light or universal stop is not required; bounded isolation and escalation are the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_HISTORICAL_MECHANISTIC
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S004], [S005], [S006], [S067]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S025], [S029]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S031]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** Direct comparative evidence on stop-authority effectiveness under real production pressure and false alarms remains limited.

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** WORKER_LOCAL
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Trade-off: containment reduces propagation but interrupts flow. Trigger by severity and expected loss; cheap path is flag-and-follow-up for low consequence.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-009
  - EL-023
  - EL-026
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system give abnormalities a bounded stop, containment or escalation path rather than merely record them?
  - Who may stop, who must respond, and who may restart?
  - Does throughput pressure or punishment make the nominal authority unusable?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-013 — Quality at source and defect containment

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-013
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Quality at source and defect containment
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Jidoka, Toyota SQC/TQC and in-process quality assurance.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Do not pass known defective work to the next process; build checks and correction close to creation.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** End inspection discovers defects after additional work and obscures causal context.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Downstream rework, defect multiplication, ambiguous ownership and late customer harm.
- **MECHANISM:** Place proportionate checks near creation; isolate failed output; preserve trace/evidence; repair or reject under defined criteria.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Defects become more costly or less diagnosable downstream, and source checks have useful sensitivity.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For very low-risk reversible output, use sampled or downstream validation with traceability.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Testability; clear quality criteria; containment path; no incentive to hide defects; qualified repair/release.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Smaller defect escape, faster feedback and less downstream rework.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Over-testing; brittle checks; test gaming; false confidence; quality burden shifted to operators; local pass misses system failure.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Quality at source can become blame at source; inspection does not equal process capability or causal prevention.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Expanded from physical inspection and stop to automated tests, contracts, validation gates and canary release, paired with recovery.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use risk-based source verification and containment with independent system-level assurance where local checks cannot see emergent failure.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Inspection stamps, quality gates or test suites are implementations; early trustworthy containment is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** STRONGLY_RETAINED
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S001], [S005], [S010]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S046], [S060]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S041], [S057], [S061]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should source checks be balanced against independent assurance for systemic or adversarial failures?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** WORKER_LOCAL
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-005
  - EL-006
  - EL-009
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Are defects detected and contained before avoidable downstream propagation?
  - Does a local pass actually establish system quality, or is independent assurance needed?
  - Can workers report defects without absorbing blame for structural causes?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-014 — Mistake-proofing and fail-safe constraints

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-014
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Mistake-proofing and fail-safe constraints
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Toyota/Shingo-associated poka-yoke and built-in quality; broader human-factors convergence.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Design fixtures, sequences or sensors so predictable errors are prevented or immediately exposed.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Known recurrent slips remain possible and rely on vigilance or final inspection.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Repeatable human error, wrong-part use, omitted steps and unsafe state transitions.
- **MECHANISM:** Eliminate the error opportunity, constrain invalid states, make the correct action easier, or detect the mistake before propagation.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Error mode is recurrent, well understood and cheaply preventable or detectable.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Use a clear cue or checklist for infrequent low-consequence tasks rather than hard constraint.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Accurate error model; escape/override for legitimate exceptions; human-factors testing; maintenance.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Lower defect frequency and reduced dependence on memory or vigilance.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Brittle constraints; workarounds; blocking legitimate variation; new failure modes; ignored sensor faults; operator deskilling.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** A device can freeze an incorrect process model and shift error elsewhere; not all complex failure has a local mistake.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** From physical jigs and limit switches to type systems, validation, interlocks and safe defaults.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Risk-based constraint or detection for stable error modes, with tested override, monitoring and revision.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A branded poka-yoke device is unnecessary; any verified fail-safe design may implement the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S004], [S005], [S067]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S045], [S046]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S041], [S057]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** Evidence comparing hard prevention, detection and training across complex socio-technical errors is incomplete.

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** TEAM
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-017
  - EL-022
  - EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is there a recurrent, well-understood error mode that design can remove or expose?
  - Does the constraint preserve legitimate exceptions and fail safely?
  - Would a hard control solve the cause or merely force workarounds?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-015 — Jidoka/autonomation: automate routine operation while reserving human exception judgement

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-015
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Jidoka/autonomation: automate routine operation while reserving human exception judgement
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Toyoda automatic loom and Toyota machine autonomation.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Machines operate without constant watching and stop when an abnormality occurs so one person can oversee several.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Humans are consumed by routine monitoring while automation continues producing bad output.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Unattended defect propagation and wasteful human machine-watching.
- **MECHANISM:** Automate stable routine execution and detection; stop or hand off uncertain/abnormal cases with context to a competent human.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Routine is stable enough to automate, abnormalities are detectable, and human attention is scarce or high-value.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** Keep manual work where volume is low, judgement dominates or automation cannot fail safely.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Reliable detection; safe state; human response capacity; understandable handoff; maintenance; workload monitoring.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Higher leverage of human attention, earlier containment and lower repetitive monitoring burden.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Automation bias; deskilling; opaque models; alert overload; unsafe autonomous continuation; humans left with only rare hard cases.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Automation may be justified as labour reduction and can worsen surveillance, pace or job quality; 'human touch' rhetoric is not sufficient.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Modern form resembles human-on-the-loop automation with bounded autonomy, observability and graceful degradation.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Automate only stable, testable routine work; make uncertainty and abnormality explicit; preserve human competence and safe fallback.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** Toyota-style machines, andon lights or robots are not required; bounded human-machine allocation is the property.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_HISTORICAL_MODERATE_TRANSFER
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S001], [S005], [S067]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S025], [S026], [S029]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S041], [S057]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should organisations preserve human competence when automation makes intervention rare but consequential?

**Additional adjudication fields**

- **LINEAGE_CLASSIFICATION:** DIRECT_TPS_LINEAGE
- **IMPROVEMENT_AUTHORITY:** SYSTEM_OWNER
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:**
  - EL-021
  - EL-023
  - EL-026
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is automation limited to stable routine work with safe exception handoff?
  - Can humans understand, override and recover the system?
  - Does the automation reduce harmful work or merely reduce headcount and concentrate cognitive risk?
- **DISPOSITION_NOTE:** —

### EL-016 — Process capability, variation measurement and statistical quality control

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-016
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Process capability, variation measurement and statistical quality control
- **HISTORICAL_ORIGIN:** Shared Shewhart/Deming statistical-quality ancestry; Toyota SQC from 1949 and TQC from 1961.
- **ORIGINAL_FORM:** Use control charts and statistical methods to distinguish process behaviour, pursue causes and prevent recurrence.
- **PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED:** Anecdotal reactions to variation confuse noise with assignable causes and fail to establish capability.
- **FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT:** Tampering, recurrent defects, false improvement and unstable output.
- **MECHANISM:** Define measures and sampling; examine stability and capability; distinguish common/special causes; test countermeasures over time.
- **TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT:** Repeated measurable output where variation matters and observations are sufficiently comparable.
- **NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH:** For rare or unique work, use structured case evidence and direct tests rather than force a control chart.
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement-system validity; enough observations; statistical competence; stable definitions; authority to change systemic causes.
- **EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF:** Earlier instability detection, better causal discrimination and qualified process improvement.
- **KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES:** Chart ritual; bad data; arbitrary limits; metric gaming; treating statistical control as fitness for use; ignoring rare catastrophic modes.
- **IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS:** Lean accounts sometimes appropriate quality ancestry; Deming did not originate JIT or jidoka, and exact influence on A3/5 Whys is unproven.
- **HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED:** Expanded from shop-floor control charts to reliability, experimentation and longitudinal outcome evidence.
- **MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM:** Use fit-for-purpose variation analysis and capability evidence, explicitly separating stability, customer need and risk.
- **CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY:** A control-chart poster, Deming Prize programme or Six Sigma belt is not required.
- **CURRENT_STATUS:** STRONGLY_RETAINED
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_HISTORICAL_EMPIRICAL
- **PRIMARY_SOURCES:** [S010], [S011]
- **CRITICAL_SOURCES:** [S045], [S047]
- **EMPIRICAL_OR_DOMAIN_EVIDENCE:** [S041], [S042], [S062]
- **OPEN_QUESTIONS:** How should sparse, nonstationary and dependent observations be governed without false statistical certainty?

**Additional adjudication fields**
