| Five Whys | EL-019 causal depth beyond the immediate symptom. | A cheap prompt to ask whether an apparent cause has upstream conditions. | It forces a single linear root, stops arbitrarily at five, reflects facilitator bias, omits interacting/systemic causes or selects weak controls. | NO | Fault tree, causal graph, barrier analysis, statistical study, counterfactual test, multiple-cause investigation. | Use only as an initial prompt for simple, well-bounded problems; never treat five answers as causal proof. | [S045], [S046] |
| PDCA/PDSA cycle | EL-019 iterative prediction-bearing tests and EL-020 sustainment. | A memorable loop linking proposed change, observation, study and next action. | No explicit prediction, repeated cycles, data over time, small-scale test or documented learning; labels are applied retrospectively. | NO | Controlled experiment, canary, simulation, A/B test, staged rollout, sequential decision process. | Retain disciplined iteration; cycle names and diagrams have no evidentiary force. | [S012], [S047], [S048] |
| Kaizen event / rapid-improvement event | EL-019 and EL-020 focused local improvement and sustainment. | Protected cross-functional attention, rapid observation and implementation around a bounded problem. | Event count substitutes for value; workers carry extra load; structural causes are out of scope; gains decay after facilitators leave. | NO | Embedded improvement time, incident follow-up, small funded experiment, maintenance sprint, system-owner intervention. | Trigger only for a bounded problem with authority, baseline, follow-up and sustainment economics. | [S043], [S044], [S060] |
| Gemba walk / genchi genbutsu visit | EL-018 direct observation combined with traceable evidence. | Exposure to work conditions and discrepancies hidden by aggregate reports. | Executive tourism, staged work, surveillance, anecdote dominance or local observation standing in for distributed/system data. | NO | Work sampling, trace replay, user observation, log inspection, shadowing, field test, frontline interview. | Retain direct evidence acquisition but triangulate it; presence at a location is not causal understanding. | [S068], [S020], [S029] |
| Poka-yoke device/check fixture | EL-014 mistake-proofing and fail-safe constraints. | Prevention or immediate detection of a stable, foreseeable error mode. | The model is wrong, legitimate variation is blocked, bypasses proliferate, new failure modes are hidden or automation creates complacency. | NO | Type constraint, interlock, schema validation, safe default, compatibility check, test oracle, forcing function. | Use when the error mode and safe constraint are understood and an escape/review path exists. | [S004], [S041], [S046] |
| Quality circle / suggestion scheme | EL-021 capability and EL-022 safe participation/change authority. | A channel for frontline knowledge and collective improvement. | Suggestions are counted, ignored, uncompensated or limited to worker-controlled symptoms; management retains all meaningful decisions. | NO | Team retrospection, funded improvement proposal, incident ownership, participatory design, union-management forum. | Retain real voice, resources and feedback; participation theatre is not respect. | [S006], [S013], [S025], [S026], [S031] |
| Obeya / visual war room | EL-002 integration, EL-003 shared state and EL-030 cross-functional product-development coordination. | Co-located decisions, dependency visibility and rapid cross-functional conflict resolution. | Walls become stale reporting; attendance replaces authority; remote/distributed evidence is lost; executive surveillance dominates. | NO | Integrated digital control room, decision log, dependency review, model-based engineering environment. | Use a shared decision environment only when coordination latency is material. | [S049], [S050], [S051] |
| Autonomation/jidoka equipment | EL-015 automate routine operation while reserving human exception judgement, plus EL-011 detection. | Frees people from constant watching and stops defect propagation on known abnormal states. | False alarms, unmodelled conditions, opaque automation, no responder, deskilling or unsafe override. | NO | Health check, exception monitor, circuit breaker, anomaly detector, guarded automation, human-in-the-loop review. | Automate only with bounded authority, observable state, safe fallback and competent human response. | [S001], [S067] |
| SMED workshop / setup chart | EL-006 setup/changeover latency reduction. | Separates internal/external setup and makes smaller economic batches possible. | Optimises rare changeovers with no queue benefit; shifts preparation burden elsewhere; introduces quality/safety risk. | NO | Deployment automation, fixture redesign, pre-staging, configuration-as-code, modular interface, rehearsal. | Retain setup-cost reduction where it changes batch or recovery economics; reject workshop ritual. | [S004], [S007], [S032] |
| Daily management board / tiered huddle | EL-003 current state, EL-011 abnormalities, EL-024 response ownership and EL-025 metric governance. | Short response cadence and explicit ownership of deviations. | Red/green theatre, target pressure, metric gaming, repeated escalation without structural action or meeting overload. | NO | Exception-driven alerting, operations review, incident queue, service dashboard with owner/SLA. | Use only when cadence and display shorten response; routine status recitation is ceremony. | [S020], [S025], [S030], [S058], [S059] |
| Supplier kanban / tightly synchronised deliveries | EL-010 information integrity, EL-027 supplier capability and EL-028 dependency resilience. | Lower duplicated stock and faster replenishment/quality feedback in capable, proximate relationships. | Risk is shifted to suppliers, long or variable lead times break replenishment, lower tiers are invisible, concentration creates common-mode failure. | NO | Segmented replenishment, safety stock, dual source, reserved capacity, regionalisation, supplier development, visibility platform. | Never universalise; choose per dependency criticality, turbulence, lead time and recovery option. | [S035], [S037], [S038], [S039], [S040], [S069] |
| Software kanban board | EL-004 WIP/release control, EL-002 flow visibility and EL-003 state legibility. | Shared workflow state and an explicit conversation about commitment limits. | Tickets are not comparable work, hidden work bypasses the board, blocked columns accumulate, cards are split to game cycle time or architecture remains invisible. | NO | Queue admission policy, repository state, issue/event telemetry, service ownership limits, dependency graph. | Board optional; retain WIP/queue decisions only when item identity and workflow state are meaningful. | [S054], [S055], [S056], [S059] |
| DORA or cycle-time dashboard | EL-025 proxy governance and EL-031 software delivery flow/reliability. | System-level signals about throughput, stability and recovery that can expose bottlenecks. | Targets induce gaming, work selection changes, local teams are ranked, customer outcomes are ignored or measurement definitions drift. | NO | Balanced outcome/reliability evidence, sampled delivery review, service-level objectives, incident/experiment evidence. | Use as diagnostic signals with guardrails, never as individual productivity quotas or proof of value. | [S058], [S059] |
| Set-based matrix / trade-off curve | EL-029 option preservation and EL-030 reusable trade-off knowledge. | Makes feasible ranges, constraints and elimination evidence explicit before irreversible convergence. | Alternatives multiply without value, decision rules are absent, evidence is weak or the choice is cheap/reversible. | NO | Design-space model, option register, simulation envelope, architecture decision matrix, staged prototype portfolio. | Use only under material uncertainty and irreversible coupling; exact Toyota artefacts are not required. | [S050], [S052] |

## EVOLVED_LEAN_CRITICISM_LEDGER

| CRITICISM_ID | CRITICISM | TARGET | CLASSIFICATION | EVIDENCE | VALID_CORE | EVOLVED_DISPOSITION | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CR-01 | Lean universalism and the caricature of mass production | LEAN_GENERALISATION | CONTEXT_MISMATCH | Early critics disputed IMVP's economic reasoning, plant comparisons and universal-best-way claims; later genealogy and contingency work narrowed the claim. | Cross-plant configurations can outperform alternatives under some conditions. | Reject universal superiority; retain mechanism-by-mechanism, context-bound evaluation. | [S017], [S024], [S019], [S023] |
| CR-02 | Tool copying without the management and capability system | MANAGEMENT_ADOPTION | CEREMONIAL_TOOL_COPYING | Later TPS interpretations, construct studies and implementation research find that visible tools do not constitute the system and soft practices/culture distinguish outcomes. | Some tools are cheap implementations of real properties. | Require failure mode, mechanism, preconditions, owner and outcome; exact tool adoption has NO_GENERAL_PROPERTY. | [S020], [S021], [S022], [S030] |
| CR-03 | 'Lean and mean' cost cutting, layoffs and trust destruction | MANAGEMENT_ADOPTION / LABOUR_EFFECT | LABOUR_OR_SOCIO_TECHNICAL_EFFECT | Toyota's own history records 1950 job-cut conflict; labour research links some Lean deployments to reduced autonomy, intensification and poorer well-being. | Productivity and avoidable-cost reduction can be legitimate. | Treat employment security, fair gain sharing and trust as causal preconditions where workers must expose and remove work; do not relabel harmful deployments counterfeit by definition. | [S014], [S025], [S026], [S028], [S029], [S070] |
| CR-04 | Worker participation rhetoric without decision authority | TPS_PROPERTY / MANAGEMENT_ADOPTION | LABOUR_OR_SOCIO_TECHNICAL_EFFECT | Toyota doctrine and participant accounts emphasise participation, while longitudinal and review evidence shows participation and autonomy can decline. | Frontline knowledge and safe signalling are engineering resources. | Separate suggestion, signal, local change, cross-functional change and system-owner authority; require feedback, protection and resources. | [S006], [S013], [S015], [S026], [S027], [S031] |
| CR-05 | Coercive standardisation and deskilling | TPS_PROPERTY | LABOUR_OR_SOCIO_TECHNICAL_EFFECT | Standardised work can support visibility and training, but longitudinal evidence links formalisation/assembly Lean to reduced autonomy and skill use. | An explicit current baseline enables comparison and learning. | Retain only versioned baselines with exceptions, worker input, controlled revision, review and retirement; reject immutable compliance. | [S004], [S020], [S026], [S031] |
| CR-06 | Low inventory and JIT brittleness | TPS_PROPERTY / LEAN_GENERALISATION | RESILIENCE_FAILURE | Operations science requires buffering variability; inventory-performance can be concave; disruption cases expose tier opacity and long recovery. | Excess inventory can hide instability and lengthen feedback. | Use an explicit inventory/capacity/time/information/redundancy portfolio and segment JIT/JIC by criticality, lead time and turbulence. | [S032], [S033], [S035], [S037], [S039], [S040] |
| CR-07 | Supplier synchronisation as cost and risk transfer | TPS_PROPERTY / MANAGEMENT_ADOPTION | RESILIENCE_FAILURE | Toyota's network enabled knowledge sharing, but disruptions exposed multi-tier dependence; empirical work finds JIT disruption risk depends on integration and context. | Joint capability and timely information can reduce defects and duplicated buffers. | Retain fair supplier development and multi-tier visibility; reject universal concentration, single sourcing and buyer-imposed inventory removal. | [S038], [S035], [S037], [S039], [S040], [S069] |
| CR-08 | Maximum utilisation and local efficiency worsen flow | LEAN_GENERALISATION / METRIC | LOCAL_OPTIMISATION | Queueing/Factory Physics distinguishes throughput from utilisation and shows variability must be buffered; local utilisation can create WIP and delay. | Idle capacity can sometimes be avoidable cost. | Optimise end-to-end outcome and queue behaviour; govern strategic slack explicitly rather than eliminate it. | [S032], [S033] |
| CR-09 | Takt and levelling fail under high variety or unstable demand | TPS_PROPERTY | CONTEXT_MISMATCH | Classic levelling presumes repetitive measurable work; high-mix/low-volume research requires grouping, workload control and more adaptive planning. | Smoothing avoidable variability and aligning capacity to demand remain useful. | Treat takt/heijunka as manufacturing-context implementations; level only the stable component and buffer residual variability. | [S004], [S005], [S034], [S063], [S064] |
| CR-10 | Manufacturing metaphors distort discovery and knowledge work | LEAN_GENERALISATION / SOFTWARE_TRANSLATION | CONTEXT_MISMATCH | Product-development and software literatures show uncertain, non-linear work, heterogeneous items and architectural dependencies; set-based design may require deliberate parallelism. | Queues, feedback delay and excessive commitment also exist in knowledge work. | Retain decision/feedback mechanisms; reject forced factory routing, task interchangeability and physical-inventory analogy. | [S050], [S052], [S053], [S055], [S059] |
| CR-11 | Kaizen and improvement-event theatre | MANAGEMENT_ADOPTION | IMPLEMENTATION_FAILURE | Event research identifies leadership, goal clarity, follow-up and work-area ownership as sustainment conditions; healthcare evidence shows local gains need not improve system outcomes. | Protected local experimentation can solve bounded problems. | Require baseline, authority, countermeasure qualification, follow-up, economic payoff and retirement; event count is rejected. | [S043], [S044], [S060], [S061] |
| CR-12 | A3, Five Whys or PDCA templates substitute for causal inquiry | A_SPECIFIC_TOOL | IMPLEMENTATION_FAILURE | Five Whys can create arbitrary single-root stories; RCA often stops at weak controls; PDSA studies frequently lack iterative cycles, predictions and data over time. | Compact reasoning and iterative testing can be valuable. | Retain evidence-bound causal models, explicit predictions and qualified countermeasures; no named template has intrinsic evidentiary force. | [S045], [S046], [S047], [S048] |
| CR-13 | Visual management becomes surveillance or stale reporting | A_SPECIFIC_TOOL / MANAGEMENT_ADOPTION | METRIC_GAMING | Worker studies show control/intensification risks; 5S and dashboard evidence is heterogeneous and implementation-dependent. | Legible current state can lower search and response delay. | Use minimum action-linked visibility with freshness and access controls; prohibit individual ranking and ritual scorekeeping. | [S025], [S026], [S066], [S059] |
| CR-14 | Cycle time, WIP, defects and productivity proxies are gamed | METRIC | METRIC_GAMING | Lean construct ambiguity and modern developer-productivity research warn that single metrics omit satisfaction, collaboration, activity quality and outcomes. | Operational measures can expose system constraints. | Use balanced diagnostic signals, stable definitions, gaming checks and outcome/reliability constraints; never individual quotas. | [S021], [S022], [S058], [S059] |
| CR-15 | Efficiency removes slack needed for learning, maintenance and recovery | TPS_PROPERTY / LEAN_GENERALISATION | RESILIENCE_FAILURE | Factory Physics explicitly describes capacity buffers, including preventive-maintenance and problem-solving capacity; reliability studies show TPM contributes to performance. | Unowned slack can become hidden delay or excess cost. | Name buffer purpose, owner, trigger and review; remove accidental queues, preserve justified slack. | [S032], [S041], [S042] |
| CR-16 | Stop-the-line creates false stops, alarm fatigue or production avoidance | TPS_PROPERTY | CONTEXT_MISMATCH / IMPLEMENTATION_FAILURE | Toyota's fixed-position system used response windows and small buffers; stop authority depends on stability and support rather than an unconditional binary stop. | Early containment prevents defect propagation. | Separate detect, signal, triage, contain, stop, restore and learn; calibrate thresholds and require responder capacity and non-punitive use. | [S004], [S005], [S067], [S031] |
| CR-17 | Respect-for-people doctrine diverges from observed practice | TPS_PROPERTY / LABOUR_EFFECT | LABOUR_OR_SOCIO_TECHNICAL_EFFECT | Primary doctrine is explicit, yet insider, health and longitudinal research reports strain, limited autonomy and work intensification in some contexts. | Mutual trust, development and safe voice can enable learning. | Treat respect claims as testable work-design conditions, not an authenticity shield or presumed benefit. | [S006], [S013], [S015], [S025], [S026], [S028], [S029], [S070] |
| CR-18 | Transformations decay after leadership turnover or facilitator withdrawal | MANAGEMENT_ADOPTION | IMPLEMENTATION_FAILURE | Kaizen sustainment and implementation studies identify management commitment, ownership, culture and follow-up as decisive; tool presence is insufficient. | Improvement can be institutionalised. | Give each change an owner, operating evidence, review cadence, maintenance cost and retirement rule; audit persistence after attention leaves. | [S030], [S043], [S044] |
| CR-19 | Local process gains are presented as customer, patient or business outcomes | LEAN_GENERALISATION | LOCAL_OPTIMISATION | Manufacturing meta-analysis reports average operational/business gains with heterogeneity; healthcare reviews find waiting/flow gains but weak or mixed patient, cost and worker outcomes. | Process measures can be causal intermediates. | Require an explicit outcome chain and measure adverse transfer, durability and beneficiary-level effect. | [S060], [S061], [S062] |
| CR-20 | Set-based design becomes expensive parallelism | LEAN_PRODUCT_DEVELOPMENT_PROPERTY | CONTEXT_MISMATCH | The literature supports option-range reasoning but practical implementation and comparative causal evidence remain limited. | Preserving alternatives can prevent premature irreversible commitment. | Trigger only when uncertainty, coupling and reversal cost justify the option portfolio; specify elimination evidence and budget. | [S050], [S051], [S052] |
| CR-21 | Software Kanban is board adoption without engineering capability | SOFTWARE_TRANSLATION | CEREMONIAL_TOOL_COPYING | The SLR reports benefits but an early, case-heavy evidence base and organisational challenges; WIP limits alone do not create automated qualification, architecture or recovery. | Commitment limits and workflow visibility can shorten feedback. | Combine WIP decisions with automation, observability, reliability, recovery and value evidence; board presence proves nothing. | [S055], [S056], [S057], [S058], [S059] |
| CR-22 | Waste language hides externalities and necessary redundancy | LEAN_GENERALISATION | CONTEXT_MISMATCH | Waste/value definitions vary; worker, supplier, resilience and public costs may disappear under a narrow customer/cost frame. | Avoidable non-value activity should be challenged. | Supersede universal waste elimination with outcome, constraint, risk-transfer and buffer economics. | [S024], [S025], [S032], [S040], [S065] |

## EVOLVED_LEAN_EVOLUTION_UNDER_CRITICISM

| CRITICISM | WHAT_HAPPENED_NEXT | CLASSIFICATION | SURVIVING_CHANGE | RESIDUAL_RISK | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Lean reduced to visible tools | System and learning interpretations foregrounded specification, connections, pathways, soft practices and culture. | REFINED | Tools became optional implementations of underlying properties. | The 'true Lean' defence can still immunise the doctrine from falsification. | [S020], [S021], [S022], [S030] |
| Universal best-way claims | Genealogy and contingency literature narrowed claims and documented definitional plurality. | NARROWED | Context-specific mechanisms, not universal Toyota imitation. | Consulting and transformation rhetoric remains universalist. | [S019], [S023], [S024] |
| Waste elimination as master objective | Lean Thinking added value and flow; operations science reframed Lean around buffer cost and variability; resilience work added risk. | SUPERSEDED | Outcome-constrained removal of avoidable loss. | 'Waste' remains manager-defined and can absorb resilience or labour capacity. | [S018], [S032], [S040], [S065] |
| Low inventory brittleness | Inventory-performance studies and disruptions led to explicit buffer portfolios, multi-tier mapping and JIT/JIC segmentation. | HYBRIDISED | Conditional inventory/WIP reduction after capacity, time and recovery design. | Segmentation rules and disruption probabilities remain difficult to estimate. | [S033], [S035], [S036], [S037], [S039], [S040] |
| Worker strain and pseudo-participation | Respect, culture, support and soft practices received stronger explicit emphasis; empirical work remained mixed. | REFINED / STILL_CONTESTED | Safe voice, capability, security and fair gains as causal preconditions. | No label guarantees autonomy, health or bargaining power. | [S015], [S025], [S026], [S027], [S028], [S030], [S070] |
| Immutable standardisation | Learning-system interpretations reframed standards as explicit hypotheses/baselines; evolved form adds versioning, exceptions and retirement. | REFINED | Observable current baseline with controlled revision. | Deployed practice may still be coercive; expiry/retirement are later safeguards rather than universal historic Toyota practice. | [S004], [S020], [S026], [S031] |
| Stop-the-line absolutism | Historical fixed-position stop and tiered response reveal detection, support windows, containment, local buffers and escalation. | REFINED | Bounded stop/contain/recover authority proportional to propagation risk. | False alarms, support overload and throughput pressure can suppress use. | [S004], [S005], [S067], [S031] |
| Template problem solving | Quality research demanded predictions, iterative tests, data over time, systems causation and stronger controls. | REFINED / REPLACED | Evidence-bound causal inquiry and qualified countermeasures. | A3/PDCA language can still disguise retrospective narrative. | [S045], [S046], [S047], [S048] |
| Kaizen activity without durable payoff | Sustainment research emphasised ownership, leadership, follow-up and work-area conditions. | REFINED | Improvement economics, maintenance, expiry and retirement. | Long-term causal evidence remains thinner than event-level evidence. | [S043], [S044], [S060] |
| Factory model cannot handle product uncertainty | Lean product development preserved alternatives, front-loaded learning and cross-functional trade-off knowledge. | DOMAIN_SPECIFIC / HYBRIDISED | Set-based option preservation under uncertainty and irreversible coupling. | Parallel exploration can be wasteful; implementation evidence is uneven. | [S049], [S050], [S051], [S052] |
| Factory pull does not map cleanly to software | Software Kanban abstracted pull to workflow/WIP and evolutionary change; Continuous Delivery added automation and release safety. | GENERALIZED / HYBRIDISED | Conditional commitment limits plus automated qualification and recovery. | Ticket metrics and board state can be gamed; discovery and architecture remain under-modelled. | [S053], [S054], [S055], [S056], [S057] |
| Speed-only software flow | DevOps/DORA paired throughput with stability/recovery; SPACE rejected one-dimensional developer productivity. | HYBRIDISED | Flow constrained by reliability, observability, recovery and human factors. | Observational evidence and proxy gaming limit causal and evaluative certainty. | [S058], [S059] |
| Supplier coordination creates fragility | Toyota and scholarship expanded multi-tier mapping, knowledge sharing, disruption preparation and segment-specific resilience. | HYBRIDISED | Dependency visibility, joint capability and fair risk allocation. | Focal buyers may still externalise inventory, capacity and labour cost. | [S035], [S036], [S037], [S038], [S039], [S040] |
| Local gains do not establish service/health outcomes | Systematic reviews separated patient flow/process outcomes from patient, worker and financial outcomes. | NARROWED | Use queue/error mechanisms only with domain-specific outcome chains and safeguards. | Heterogeneous interventions and weak studies leave attribution unresolved. | [S060], [S061], [S062] |
| 5S/visual control as universal hygiene | Evidence supports some local organisation and safety benefits but remains small and heterogeneous. | NARROWED | Work-state legibility where search or ambiguity is material. | Audit scoring, surveillance and decay. | [S066], [S025], [S028] |

## EVOLVED_LEAN_FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILES

The governing correction is that variability cannot be eliminated by decree. It is absorbed through some combination of inventory, capacity and time, supplemented in real systems by information quality, redundancy, scope flexibility and recovery options. Removing one buffer without choosing another usually moves the cost into lateness, lost service, overtime, fragility or hidden queueing. `[S032]`

| PROPERTY | VALUE_MECHANISM | VARIABILITY_ASSUMPTIONS | BUFFER_ASSUMPTIONS | FAILURE_IF_OVER_APPLIED | RESILIENCE_TRADEOFF | CHEAP_PATH | MATURE_FORM | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EL-002 — End-to-end flow/queue/handoff visibility | Reveals where elapsed time, rework and dependency waiting accumulate so local gains can be judged against total outcome. | Items and states are traceable enough for observed paths to represent the system; exploratory loops are modelled rather than forced into a line. | Queues may be legitimate buffers; visibility precedes removal. | Instrumentation cost, map theatre, false linearity, boundary explosion and surveillance. | A slower path may carry redundancy, review or safety value; latency is not automatically waste. | Sample a few representative items or incidents before building a persistent map. | Decision-useful path model with queues, feedback loops, work classes, ownership and outcome constraints. | [S018], [S020], [S032], [S061] |
| EL-004 — Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits | Prevents uncontrolled admission, reduces congestion and shortens feedback by placing an explicit bound on committed work. | Arrival, service-time and priority variation are observable enough to tune the bound; critical exceptions have a policy. | WIP is one buffer; reducing it may require capacity or time slack and cannot eliminate variability. | Starvation, hidden pre-entry queues, blocked-work accumulation, priority inversion and card/ticket gaming. | Very tight limits improve focus but reduce ability to absorb bursts or maintain parallel recovery paths. | Use a simple 'finish before starting' rule or one team-level commitment cap; no board required. | Class-aware admission control calibrated to demand, capacity, lead time and service risk, with explicit override evidence. | [S003], [S006], [S032], [S055], [S056] |
| EL-005 — Batch-size economics and small reversible increments | Shortens feedback, lowers defect/change blast radius and reduces inventory tied up between decisions. | Smaller transfers yield information before the next irreversible commitment; items can be partitioned without losing essential integrity. | Setup, validation and coordination overhead are real counter-buffers; batch size cannot be chosen independently of them. | Excessive transaction cost, fragmentary outputs, test overhead, context switching and loss of economies of scale. | Small increments aid rollback, but too little batching can remove redundancy or coordinated end-to-end validation. | Split only the next high-risk decision or release rather than decompose all work. | Choose batch and transfer size by setup cost, feedback value, coupling, failure blast radius and reversibility. | [S004], [S032], [S033], [S057] |
| EL-006 — Setup/changeover/release/feedback latency reduction | Reduces the fixed cost that makes large batches economical and accelerates detection, recovery and learning. | The setup or release delay is a material component of total latency and is reducible without disproportionate risk. | Faster change/release can substitute for inventory or time buffers, but qualification and safety capacity must remain. | Optimising rare setup, moving work off-book, unsafe shortcuts or automation maintenance exceeding benefit. | Automation can speed recovery but creates toolchain dependencies and common-mode failure. | Pre-stage, checklist or automate the single slowest/repeated step before building a full platform. | Reduce fixed transition and feedback costs while preserving independent qualification, fallback and economic review. | [S004], [S007], [S032], [S057] |
| EL-007 — Demand-capacity pacing and production levelling | Smooths avoidable load oscillation, exposes mismatch and reduces batch/schedule amplification in repetitive work. | A stable aggregate demand component and reasonably bounded processing-time/mix variation exist. | Residual variation is absorbed by capacity, time, finished stock or service policy; levelling does not make it vanish. | Backlog, overtime, supplier oscillation, coercive pacing and poor response to rare or high-variety demand. | Smoothing aids stability but can reduce responsiveness to real regime changes. | Level only a recurring work class or reserve a small capacity band instead of imposing universal takt. | Adaptive demand-capacity envelope with explicit residual-variation buffers and exception classes. | [S004], [S005], [S034], [S063], [S064] |
| EL-008 — Explicit buffer portfolio and strategic slack | Chooses how variability and disruption will be absorbed rather than allowing hidden queues, lateness or cancelled demand to become the default buffer. | Material variability and failure modes can be characterised at least qualitatively; not all sources are removable. | Inventory, capacity and time are core buffers; information, redundancy, scope flexibility and recovery options may supplement them. | Unreviewed inventory, idle capacity, bureaucratic contingency plans and resilience theatre. | More protection costs money and can hide problems; less protection raises tail-loss and recovery risk. | For each critical variability source, name the current buffer and owner before adding anything. | Governed portfolio with purpose, activation trigger, service/risk target, owner, cost, adequacy test and review/retirement date. | [S032], [S033], [S035], [S037], [S040] |
| EL-009 — Flow/queue-aware capacity over maximum local utilisation | Preserves service and throughput under variability by avoiding queue explosion near full utilisation. | Arrivals or service times vary; shared resources create queue interactions. | Capacity slack is an intentional buffer whose cost is compared with delay, lost throughput and failure cost. | Excess capacity without purpose, underinvestment in variability reduction or political protection of idle resources. | Slack improves burst response, maintenance and learning but can lower apparent unit utilisation. | Stop treating utilisation as a target; first monitor queue growth and missed service at current load. | Capacity policy based on variability, queue/service target, maintenance, recovery and economic loss—not maximum busy time. | [S032], [S042] |
| EL-010 — Demand and replenishment information integrity | Prevents false pull, bullwhip, duplicate production and stockout caused by delayed or corrupted consumption signals. | Signal latency, error, cancellation and forecast uncertainty can be measured or bounded. | Information quality can reduce but never eliminate physical/time/capacity buffers. | Over-centralised data, brittle real-time coupling, privacy/surveillance, automated amplification of bad signals. | Fast synchronisation can increase common-mode reaction to a shared false signal. | Validate the few signals that authorise costly or irreversible work; retain manual exception confirmation. | Provenance-bearing demand signal with latency, confidence, cancellation and override semantics. | [S002], [S003], [S005], [S032], [S040] |
| EL-026 — Reliability and maintainability | Reduces internal variability, unplanned stops and the buffer burden needed to maintain flow. | Failure modes recur enough to justify preventive, predictive or design action. | Maintenance windows, spares and responder capacity are legitimate buffers; zero downtime is not assumed. | Over-maintenance, sensor noise, costly redundancy or optimisation for availability rather than mission outcome. | Planned downtime and spare capacity reduce nominal utilisation but improve recovery and tail performance. | Stabilise the dominant recurrent failure and define a restoration runbook before deploying predictive infrastructure. | Risk-based reliability programme linking failure mode, detection, maintainability, recovery, spare/skill capacity and learning. | [S032], [S041], [S042] |
| EL-028 — Multi-tier dependency visibility and resilience segmentation | Identifies critical lower-tier concentration, lead time and recovery constraints before disruption propagates. | Supplier/dependency topology and criticality can be mapped sufficiently; common-mode risks change over time. | Different segments justify different mixes of stock, capacity, alternate source, regionalisation and recovery agreement. | Mapping burden, false confidence, supplier surveillance, frozen classifications and duplication with no feasible switch path. | Diversity and redundancy cost more and can weaken learning economies; concentration improves coordination but raises correlated risk. | Map only critical single points of failure and the first unknown tier; test one recovery scenario. | Criticality- and turbulence-based segmentation with multi-tier provenance, viable recovery options, fair cost allocation and periodic stress tests. | [S035], [S036], [S037], [S039], [S040] |
| EL-031 — Software delivery flow with automation, observability, reliability and recovery | Small, qualified changes shorten feedback and reduce release blast radius while observability and recovery preserve service. | Changes differ in risk and coupling; telemetry and automated tests provide partial—not complete—evidence. | Deployment capacity, rollback paths, feature controls, on-call recovery and architectural decoupling are buffers/capabilities. | Shipping low-value work faster, flaky automation, metric gaming, alert fatigue, underfunded architecture and unsafe continuous change. | Higher change frequency can improve recovery and learning but increases exposure unless qualification and rollback are reliable. | Automate one repeated release check and create a tested rollback for the highest-risk service before adopting a delivery framework. | Risk-tiered small changes with automated evidence, observability, safe release controls, reliability objectives, human factors and verified recovery. | [S053], [S055], [S056], [S057], [S058], [S059] |

### Frozen flow propositions

```text
less WIP != better system in every operating region
higher utilisation != higher throughput under variability
lower inventory != lower total expected risk
faster local cycle time != better end-to-end outcome
pull signal exists != demand information is trustworthy
smallest possible batch != lowest total cost
visible queue != understood cause
strategic slack != accidental waiting
```


## EVOLVED_LEAN_BUILT_IN_QUALITY_AND_ABNORMALITY_MODEL

| STAGE | PURPOSE | MINIMUM_EVIDENCE | AUTHORITY | FAILURE_MODE | RELATED_PROPERTIES | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. DEFINE NORMAL/ABNORMAL | State the expected condition, tolerance and consequence class. | Current baseline, quality/safety boundary and known exceptions. | SYSTEM_OWNER / TEAM | No stable reference; every deviation is subjective or every variation becomes an alarm. | EL-016, EL-017 | [S004], [S010], [S020] |
| 2. DETECT | Recognise deviation close enough to the source to prevent costly propagation. | Detector coverage, latency, false-negative/false-positive behaviour and observability. | WORKER_LOCAL / AUTOMATED_BOUNDED | Undetected defect, noisy monitor, hidden detector failure or over-trust in automation. | EL-011, EL-014, EL-015 | [S001], [S067] |
| 3. SIGNAL | Transfer the abnormal state, location and urgency to a responsible responder. | Named recipient, acknowledgement path, freshness and signal provenance. | WORKER_LOCAL / TEAM | Alarm without owner, stigma for signalling, stale board or alert flood. | EL-003, EL-011, EL-022 | [S004], [S005], [S031], [S067] |
| 4. TRIAGE | Classify severity, propagation risk, scope and whether continuation is safe. | Consequence model, affected population/work, uncertainty and decision threshold. | TEAM / SUPERVISORY / CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Every alert stops everything; serious defects are normalised; throughput pressure biases classification. | EL-012, EL-024 | [S005], [S067] |
| 5. CONTAIN OR STOP | Prevent additional defective/unsafe work from escaping while preserving unaffected operation when possible. | Containment boundary, stop scope, expected propagation and safe-state criterion. | WORKER_LOCAL for signal; predefined TEAM/SYSTEM authority for stop scope | Detected but not contained; unnecessary global stop; hidden rework queue; unsafe override. | EL-012, EL-013 | [S004], [S005], [S067] |
| 6. RESTORE OR PROVIDE SAFE ALTERNATIVE | Recover service without erasing the abnormality or creating uncontrolled debt. | Restoration test, fallback/rollback, temporary-control owner and expiry. | TEAM / SYSTEM_OWNER | Reset-and-forget, repeated workaround, premature restart or customer exposure. | EL-012, EL-020, EL-026, EL-031 | [S042], [S057], [S058] |
| 7. DIAGNOSE SYSTEM CAUSATION | Explain why the control environment allowed the failure, including interacting and organisational causes. | Competing hypotheses, causal evidence, boundary/assumption record and uncertainty. | CROSS_FUNCTIONAL | Single arbitrary root, operator blame, five-why story or local cause standing in for system cause. | EL-018, EL-019, EL-024 | [S045], [S046] |
| 8. DESIGN COUNTERMEASURE | Change a controllable mechanism at the strongest feasible level of the control hierarchy. | Mechanism, prediction, side effects, owner, cost and rollback. | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT by blast radius | Training/reminder chosen by default, cause/control mismatch, burden transferred to worker or supplier. | EL-014, EL-019, EL-024, EL-027 | [S046], [S047] |
| 9. QUALIFY AND RESTART | Test that the countermeasure controls the failure without unacceptable new risk and that restart conditions hold. | Pre-specified success/failure criteria, repeated or staged test, data over time where appropriate, independent check for high consequence. | TEAM / CROSS_FUNCTIONAL / SYSTEM_OWNER | One successful run treated as proof, no prediction, no regression evidence or authority ambiguity. | EL-019, EL-031 | [S047], [S048], [S057] |
| 10. UPDATE, MONITOR, EXPIRE OR RETIRE | Integrate verified learning, watch recurrence and remove temporary or obsolete controls. | Versioned baseline, recurrence measure, review date, maintenance burden and retirement test. | SYSTEM_OWNER with worker/team input | Lessons recorded but baseline unchanged; alert/control accretion; stale workaround becomes permanent. | EL-017, EL-020 | [S043], [S044], [S047] |

### Required distinctions

- `defect detected != defect contained`
- `signal emitted != responsible responder acknowledged`
- `line or service stopped != root cause understood`
- `service restored != countermeasure qualified`
- `countermeasure passed once != recurrence risk controlled`
- `worker may signal != worker is safe from blame or retaliation`
- `automation detected a modelled condition != unmodelled failure is safe`
- `low false-negative rate != tolerable alert burden`
- `local containment != end-to-end customer or system protection`
- `restart authorised != temporary control can remain indefinitely`

### Frozen interpretation

A physical andon cord is not retained as a general property. What survives is a **staged abnormality-control architecture**: define normality, detect, signal, triage, contain or stop at the narrowest safe scope, restore safely, diagnose system causation, design and qualify a countermeasure, restart under authority, and update or retire controls. The design must account for false alarms, alarm burden, propagation cost, response latency, production pressure and human safety. `[S001] [S004] [S005] [S031] [S067]`


## EVOLVED_LEAN_STANDARD_WORK_AND_LEARNING_MODEL

| STEP | REQUIREMENT | EVIDENCE_BEFORE_CHANGE | AUTHORITY | FAILURE_IF_MISSING | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. SELECT SCOPE | Use a baseline only where repeatability, consequence, training or comparison value justifies it. | Failure/variation or teaching need; avoid standardising exploratory judgement prematurely. | SYSTEM_OWNER / TEAM | Paperwork for trivial or unstable work. | [S004], [S026] |
| 2. OBSERVE ACTUAL WORK | Record how work is performed, including variants, workarounds, constraints and tacit judgement. | Direct observation plus trace/log evidence. | WORKER_LOCAL + TEAM | Management fantasy becomes the standard. | [S020], [S068], [S029] |
| 3. STATE PURPOSE AND EXPECTED OUTCOME | Connect the baseline to quality, safety, flow or learning—not obedience. | Named failure mode and expected outcome. | TEAM / SYSTEM_OWNER | Compliance can rise while performance falls. | [S018], [S020] |
| 4. VERSION THE CURRENT BASELINE | Identify scope, revision, owner, prerequisites, sequence/constraints and applicable work class. | Current process capability and known operating envelope. | TEAM / CROSS_FUNCTIONAL | Competing unwritten standards and no reliable comparison. | [S004], [S008] |
| 5. RECORD EXCEPTIONS AND EXPERT DISCRETION | Specify when the baseline does not apply and who may depart safely. | Known variability, edge cases and risk classification. | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Expert judgement is suppressed or deviations go underground. | [S026], [S028] |
| 6. TRAIN AND DEMONSTRATE CAPABILITY | Teach purpose and method; verify performance rather than attendance. | Observed execution, comprehension and safe escalation ability. | SUPERVISORY / TEAM | Standard exists only on paper; worker blamed for system/training defects. | [S008], [S009], [S021] |
| 7. MAKE DEVIATION VISIBLE AND SAFE TO REPORT | Deviation should trigger help or inquiry, not automatic blame. | Signal use, response time, no-retaliation and workload evidence. | WORKER_LOCAL signal; TEAM response | Conformance theatre and hidden abnormalities. | [S006], [S013], [S025], [S031] |
| 8. PROPOSE CHANGE WITH A PREDICTION | State mechanism, expected outcome, affected constraints and rollback. | Observed problem/opportunity and causal rationale. | WORKER_LOCAL for bounded reversible change; otherwise scaled authority | Idea volume or preference replaces learning. | [S020], [S047] |
| 9. TEST AT PROPORTIONATE SCALE | Use the smallest safe test capable of discriminating the hypothesis. | Predefined criteria and measurement plan. | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Premature rollout or endless pilot. | [S047], [S048] |
| 10. QUALIFY AND ADOPT | Compare results with prediction, adverse effects and baseline; update version only when evidence is adequate. | Repeated/staged evidence proportional to consequence. | TEAM / CROSS_FUNCTIONAL / SYSTEM_OWNER | Anecdote becomes standard; local gain transfers harm. | [S047], [S060], [S061] |
| 11. REVIEW FRESHNESS AND MAINTENANCE COST | Assign review triggers for changed product, demand, equipment, law, architecture or evidence. | Drift, recurrence, exception frequency and upkeep burden. | SYSTEM_OWNER | Stale standard and control accumulation. | [S043], [S044] |
| 12. RETIRE OR REVERT | Remove obsolete, harmful or uneconomic baselines and temporary controls; preserve provenance. | No remaining failure mode, superior replacement, unacceptable side effect or cost. | SYSTEM_OWNER with affected-worker input | Permanent bureaucracy and zombie process. | [S044], [S048] |

### Improvement-authority model

| AUTHORITY | VALID_SCOPE | REQUIRED_GUARDRAIL | NOT_SUFFICIENT_FOR |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WORKER_LOCAL | Signal abnormality; stop personal unsafe work; execute pre-authorised reversible adjustment; propose change. | No retaliation, known envelope, traceability and rapid support. | Cross-system, regulatory, safety-critical or irreversible baseline change. |
| TEAM | Tune bounded work method, local WIP, visual state and low-blast-radius experiment. | Shared outcome/constraints and rollback. | Changes transferring load or risk outside the team. |
| SUPERVISORY | Resource immediate response, coordinate containment, training and local recovery. | Cannot suppress signals to meet output targets; escalation clock/criteria. | Structural staffing, supplier or architecture causes beyond span. |
| CROSS_FUNCTIONAL | Resolve handoffs, product/process trade-offs, systemic causes and multi-area countermeasures. | Named end-to-end outcome and affected-party representation. | Portfolio, employment or enterprise-risk commitments. |
| SYSTEM_OWNER | Set system constraints, buffers, staffing, supplier/resilience policy, adoption and retirement. | Evidence, accountability for externalities and review of worker/customer impact. | Unilateral override of legal, safety or independent assurance authority. |
| CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Scaled by reversibility, blast radius, uncertainty and consequence. | Explicit authority matrix before the abnormality or experiment. | Vague 'everyone owns quality' with no decision rights. |

### Frozen interpretation

The retained property is not procedural immutability. It is an **explicit current baseline** whose identity, purpose, scope, assumptions, exceptions and expected outcome are observable. Changes require evidence proportional to consequence and authority proportional to blast radius. Review, expiry and retirement are evolved anti-bureaucracy safeguards; they are not asserted as universal historical Toyota rules. `[S004] [S008] [S020] [S026] [S031] [S043] [S044]`


## EVOLVED_LEAN_INTERNAL_TENSIONS

| TENSION | SIDE_A_PROPERTY | SIDE_B_PROPERTY | CONTEXT_THAT_FAVOURS_A | CONTEXT_THAT_FAVOURS_B | KNOWN_HYBRID_RESOLUTION | UNRESOLVED_RISK | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Efficiency versus resilience | EL-006/EL-009 reduction of delay and excess cost | EL-008/EL-028 strategic buffers and redundancy | Frequent, low-consequence, rapidly recoverable work with stable supply and measurable carrying cost. | High-consequence failure, correlated disruption, long recovery or opaque dependencies. | Segment by criticality; remove accidental queues while funding named capacity, stock, alternate-source and recovery buffers. | Tail probabilities and common-mode failure are hard to estimate. | [S032], [S033], [S035], [S040] |
| Low WIP versus strategic slack | EL-004 commitment/WIP control | EL-008 capacity/time/option buffers | Congestion, multitasking and delayed feedback dominate. | Burst response, parallel recovery or exploration requires ready capacity/options. | Limit committed execution WIP while reserving explicit response and discovery capacity outside the queue. | Organisations may relabel hidden WIP as 'slack' or cut genuine slack as 'unused capacity'. | [S032], [S029], [S057] |
| Standardisation versus expert autonomy | EL-017 explicit baseline | EL-021/EL-022 capability and judgement | Stable repetitive work, safety-critical sequence, training and high recurrence. | Novel, ambiguous, rapidly changing or highly contextual work. | Versioned baseline with explicit discretion/exception envelope and evidence-based revision rights. | Power asymmetry can make nominal change authority unreal. | [S004], [S020], [S026], [S028] |
| Flow versus exploration | EL-002/EL-004/EL-005 fast end-to-end feedback | EL-029 option preservation | Known solution path, low uncertainty, reversible delivery. | High design uncertainty, strong coupling and costly irreversible choices. | Limit execution WIP but maintain a budgeted evidence-generating option set until discriminating information arrives. | Parallel work may continue after its option value expires. | [S050], [S052], [S055] |
| Pull versus long-lead planning | EL-004/EL-010 demand-triggered release | EL-007/EL-008 advance capacity and inventory planning | Short replenishment latency and reliable capacity. | Long lead-time materials, seasonal demand, regulated capacity or rare skills. | Place a decoupling point: forecast/plan upstream where lead time demands it, pull downstream with explicit WIP. | Bad interface placement transfers forecast error or stockout risk. | [S032], [S040] |
| Local kaizen versus system optimisation | EL-019 local countermeasure capability | EL-024/EL-025 system ownership and whole-path metrics | Bounded cause and consequence within team control. | Bottleneck, incentive, architecture or supplier cause crosses boundaries. | Define escalation thresholds and require local changes to test end-to-end and externality effects. | System owners may become bottlenecks or use centralisation to suppress local agency. | [S020], [S043], [S044], [S060] |
| Stop authority versus false-stop cost | EL-012 containment/stop | EL-009/EL-026 continuity and service | High propagation, safety or customer harm; cheap bounded stop. | Noisy detector, low consequence, high restart cost or essential service continuity. | Tiered signal, fixed-position response window, scoped containment, small defined buffer and calibrated stop threshold. | Throughput pressure can silently move thresholds toward unsafe continuation. | [S004], [S005], [S067] |
| Productivity improvement versus employment security | EL-006/EL-009 productivity and capacity economics | EL-023 trust and fair gains | Survival constraint where alternatives and negotiated transition exist. | Continuous worker-driven improvement requires long-run candour and cooperation. | Advance no-layoff/redeployment rules, gain sharing, negotiated transition and natural-attrition planning where feasible. | Economic shocks may defeat promises; contingent workforces can bear displaced risk. | [S013], [S014], [S025], [S031] |
| Supplier synchronisation versus network robustness | EL-027 joint capability and timely replenishment | EL-028 segmentation, diversity and redundancy | Stable nearby supply, strong trust, low common-mode risk. | Critical long-lead components, geopolitical/climate risk, tier opacity or concentrated capacity. | Deep collaboration for capable sources plus tier mapping, alternate recovery and strategic stock/capacity for critical segments. | Second sources may not be truly independent or qualified at disruption scale. | [S035], [S037], [S038], [S039], [S040] |
| Waste reduction versus necessary redundancy | EL-033 outcome-constrained avoidable-loss removal | EL-008/EL-028 redundancy | Duplicated work has no information, safety or recovery value. | Independent verification, fault tolerance, learning options or surge/recovery capacity are valuable. | Require a purpose and adequacy test for redundancy; remove it only after a stronger control exists. | Benefits often appear only in rare events and are undervalued by short-horizon accounting. | [S032], [S040], [S065] |
| Continuous improvement versus improvement fatigue | EL-019 ongoing learning | EL-020 sustainment and bounded improvement economics | Material recurrent loss and available improvement capacity. | Stable adequate process, overloaded workforce or marginal ideas. | Budget improvement capacity, prioritise by expected loss, stop low-value campaigns and consolidate/retire controls. | Management may shift improvement labour onto workers without time or reward. | [S043], [S044], [S070] |
| Learning speed versus premature convergence | EL-005/EL-006 rapid feedback | EL-029 set-based delay of commitment | Cheap reversible experiments and clear discriminating evidence. | Evidence arrives late and decisions create irreversible architecture/tooling commitments. | Run fast tests across a bounded option set and converge only when elimination criteria are met. | Fast tests may measure proxies that favour the wrong option. | [S050], [S052], [S057] |
| Direct observation versus distributed-system evidence | EL-018 genchi genbutsu | EL-002/EL-016 traces and statistical evidence | Tacit work, ergonomic condition, user behaviour or report distortion. | Rare, distributed, latent or long-horizon failure. | Triangulate field observation with logs, samples, models and affected-party testimony. | What is visible and visitable attracts disproportionate attention. | [S068], [S020], [S029], [S032] |
| Visual transparency versus surveillance and gaming | EL-003/EL-025 legibility and metrics | EL-022/EL-023 safe voice and trust | Shared state is needed for coordination and response. | Measures can identify or punish individuals, or work is hard to compare. | Aggregate system metrics, limit access, audit gaming, prohibit individual quotas and include qualitative context. | Power holders may repurpose diagnostic data after collection. | [S025], [S026], [S058], [S059] |
| Automation versus human judgement | EL-015 autonomation and EL-031 delivery automation | EL-021 capability and EL-012 human escalation | Routine, stable, observable condition with reliable oracle and safe fallback. | Novel, ambiguous, adversarial or high-consequence exception. | Bounded automation with transparent state, human override, competency retention and tested fallback. | Automation may erode the very human skill needed during rare failure. | [S001], [S067], [S057], [S059] |
| Quality containment versus delivery speed | EL-013 source quality and EL-019 qualification | EL-005/EL-031 small-batch delivery | High defect consequence or weak downstream detection. | Low-risk reversible change with strong monitoring/rollback. | Risk-tiered evidence gates, canary release and containment proportional to consequence. | Risk tiers can be manipulated to bypass controls. | [S041], [S047], [S057], [S058] |
| Value to the customer versus labour, supplier and public constraints | EL-001 beneficiary value | EL-023/EL-027 and safety/resilience constraints | Preferences do not externalise material harm and customer outcome is legitimate. | Cost or speed gains rely on unsafe work, supplier precarity or public risk. | Define value as constrained multi-stakeholder outcome, not willingness-to-pay alone. | No neutral aggregation rule resolves all conflicts. | [S024], [S025], [S029], [S040], [S065] |

## EVOLVED_LEAN_HYBRIDISATION_PRESSURES

| PRESSURE | RELATION | LEAN_PROPERTY_AFFECTED | WHAT_WAS_IMPORTED_OR_CONVERGED | BOUNDARY | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Statistical quality and TQM | QUALITY_ANCESTRY | EL-016, EL-019, EL-021 | Control charts, variation reasoning, company-wide quality and disciplined study. | Do not credit Lean/TPS with originating the entire quality tradition or assume a PDCA label proves statistical learning. | [S010], [S011], [S012], [S041] |
| Operations science / queueing / Factory Physics | CONVERGENT_PROPERTY | EL-004, EL-005, EL-007, EL-008, EL-009 | Formal WIP definition of pull and inventory/capacity/time buffer economics under variability. | Analytical models abstract from labour, power, safety and meaning of work; combine, do not substitute. | [S032], [S033], [S034] |
| Socio-technical and labour research | REACTION_TO_LEAN / HYBRID_RESOLUTION | EL-021, EL-022, EL-023, EL-024 | Autonomy, workload, job design, trust, health, support and distribution of gains as causal constraints. | Respect rhetoric cannot stand in for measured worker outcomes or bargaining power. | [S025], [S026], [S027], [S028], [S029], [S070] |
| Lean product development | DOMAIN_TRANSLATION | EL-029, EL-030 | Set-based alternatives, front-loaded learning, cross-functional integration and reusable trade-off knowledge. | Not a direct transplant of repetitive factory flow; comparative causal evidence remains limited. | [S049], [S050], [S051], [S052] |
| Software Kanban | ADAPTIVE_IMPORT_OR_EXPORT / DOMAIN_TRANSLATION | EL-002, EL-003, EL-004 | Workflow visibility, explicit WIP and evolutionary policy change. | Cards/tickets are heterogeneous knowledge claims; a board cannot expose architecture or create testability. | [S053], [S054], [S055], [S056] |
| Continuous Delivery and DevOps | HYBRID_RESOLUTION / CONVERGENT_PROPERTY | EL-005, EL-006, EL-011, EL-012, EL-019, EL-031 | Automated qualification, small releases, observability, rollback, reliability and recovery. | Much derives independently from software engineering and operations; speed metrics remain proxies. | [S057], [S058], [S059] |
| Supply-chain resilience | REACTION_TO_LEAN / HYBRID_RESOLUTION | EL-008, EL-010, EL-027, EL-028 | Multi-tier mapping, criticality segmentation, redundancy, inventory/capacity options and recovery planning. | Neither JIT nor stockpiling alone is resilient; buffers require viable replenishment/recovery paths. | [S035], [S036], [S037], [S039], [S040] |
| Healthcare/service research | DOMAIN_TRANSLATION / CONTEXT_SWITCH | EL-001, EL-002, EL-003, EL-011, EL-019 | Patient-flow, handoff and safety visibility; domain outcome evaluation. | Professional judgement, care quality and patient outcomes cannot be reduced to factory throughput. | [S060], [S061], [S066] |
| Safety engineering and systems causation | CONVERGENT_PROPERTY / REACTION_TO_LEAN | EL-011, EL-012, EL-013, EL-019 | Multiple causation, control hierarchy, barrier reasoning and rejection of arbitrary single roots. | Five Whys and local fixes are inadequate for complex sociotechnical incidents. | [S045], [S046] |
| Reliability and maintenance engineering | QUALITY_ANCESTRY / CONVERGENT_PROPERTY | EL-008, EL-015, EL-026, EL-031 | Planned maintenance, maintainability, failure-mode control, spares, recovery and availability economics. | Nominal utilisation and inventory metrics must not erase maintenance windows or recovery capacity. | [S041], [S042], [S057] |
| Developer-productivity and metric-governance research | REACTION_TO_LEAN / HYBRID_RESOLUTION | EL-025, EL-031 | Multidimensional productivity, satisfaction, communication and performance safeguards around delivery metrics. | No single cycle-time, throughput or DORA measure should become an individual target or value claim. | [S058], [S059] |
| High-mix/low-volume planning | CONTEXT_SWITCH | EL-007, EL-004, EL-008 | Group technology, workload control and adaptive planning where classic levelling assumptions fail. | Classic takt/heijunka is manufacturing-specific and may need replacement, not cosmetic adaptation. | [S063], [S064] |

## EVOLVED_LEAN_STRONGEST_SURVIVING_PROPERTIES

These are the strongest crosswalk candidates after ceremony stripping. “Strongest” means the mechanism is historically and/or analytically clear and has substantial convergent or domain evidence; it does not mean universally triggered.

| PROPERTY_ID | PROPERTY_NAME | WHY_IT_SURVIVES | KEY_BOUNDARY | EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | KEY_SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EL-002 | End-to-end system boundary and flow/queue/handoff visibility | Maintain the cheapest decision-useful end-to-end model, including feedback/rework loops, queues, dependencies and outcome—not just touch time. | Map theatre; stale diagrams; excessive instrumentation; forced linear path for exploratory work; omission of externalities; local teams unable to change system constraints. | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S003], [S005], [S018], [S060], [S065], [S020] |
| EL-004 | Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits | Use empirical limits by work class, monitor blocked/aged work and total demand, and adapt limits with capacity, variability and service risk. | Starvation; hidden backlog before entry; arbitrary limits; gaming item size/status; blocking critical long-lead work; apparent cycle-time gains through exclusion. | HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL | [S003], [S005], [S006], [S032], [S033], [S039] |
| EL-006 | Setup, changeover, release and feedback latency reduction | Reduce total safe transition cost, including preparation, validation, recovery and burden transferred across boundaries. | Unsafe shortcuts; preparation shifted to another team; brittle automation; ergonomic or quality risk; faster release of low-value work. | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S004], [S007], [S024], [S025], [S032], [S057] |
| EL-008 | Explicit buffer portfolio and strategic slack | Optimise a portfolio of justified buffers; distinguish protective reserve from accidental queue and continuously reassess assumptions. | Slack becomes unmanaged delay; reserves are raided for utilisation; inventory obsolescence; redundancy hides defects; false quantitative precision. | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S002], [S006], [S033], [S039], [S032], [S034] |
| EL-009 | Flow and queue-aware capacity over maximum local utilisation | Optimise end-to-end service and total buffering cost; treat utilisation as a constraint or signal, not a target to maximise. | Underutilisation without purpose; politically contested spare capacity; local teams penalised for system choices; bottleneck migration. | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S003], [S005], [S024], [S025], [S032], [S056] |
| EL-011 | Abnormality detection and signalling | Detect and signal decision-relevant abnormalities with calibrated severity, accountable response and feedback on false positives/negatives. | False alarms, missed detections, alarm fatigue, ambiguous ownership, suppressed signals and throughput pressure. | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S001], [S004], [S005], [S067], [S025], [S029] |
| EL-012 | Bounded stop, containment, escalation and recovery authority | Severity-calibrated, non-punitive stop/containment authority with response time, restart criteria, evidence preservation and post-event learning. | False stops, alarm fatigue, punitive culture, ignored calls, local stop causing larger system harm, rapid reset without diagnosis. | HIGH_HISTORICAL_MECHANISTIC | [S004], [S005], [S006], [S067], [S025], [S029] |
| EL-013 | Quality at source and defect containment | Use risk-based source verification and containment with independent system-level assurance where local checks cannot see emergent failure. | Over-testing; brittle checks; test gaming; false confidence; quality burden shifted to operators; local pass misses system failure. | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S001], [S005], [S010], [S046], [S060], [S041] |
| EL-016 | Process capability, variation measurement and statistical quality control | Use fit-for-purpose variation analysis and capability evidence, explicitly separating stability, customer need and risk. | Chart ritual; bad data; arbitrary limits; metric gaming; treating statistical control as fitness for use; ignoring rare catastrophic modes. | HIGH_HISTORICAL_EMPIRICAL | [S010], [S011], [S045], [S047], [S041], [S042] |
| EL-017 | Explicit current baseline with controlled revision | A scoped, versioned, teachable baseline with exceptions, evidence, local challenge rights, qualification, expiry and retirement. | Immutable procedure; stale paperwork; coercive pace; deskilling; suppressed expert judgement; standards written by distant staff; unauthorised workarounds. | HIGH_HISTORICAL_MIXED_SOCIAL | [S004], [S008], [S009], [S025], [S026], [S028] |
| EL-019 | Evidence-bound problem solving and countermeasure qualification | A proportional causal-learning loop with competing hypotheses, predictions, safe tests, outcome/side-effect evidence and recurrence review. | A3/5-Whys ritual; confirmation bias; singular root cause; weak controls; no prediction; post hoc success; political exclusion of structural causes. | HIGH_CONVERGENT_WEAK_FORMAT_SPECIFIC | [S007], [S010], [S012], [S045], [S046], [S048] |
| EL-024 | Management responsibility for system conditions and escalation response | Leaders own the conditions they control, respond to escalations, arbitrate cross-system trade-offs and are measured on outcomes, resilience and worker effects. | Leader gemba theatre; delegation without resources; hero intervention; changing priorities; local blame; executive metrics conflict with doctrine. | MODERATE_CONVERGENT | [S005], [S010], [S013], [S024], [S029], [S030] |
| EL-026 | Equipment, service and delivery-system reliability and maintainability | Choose maintenance and recovery policy by criticality, failure distribution, consequence and replacement economics. | Maintenance theatre; over-maintenance; bad predictive models; deferred maintenance to hit throughput; hidden technical debt; single-expert dependence. | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S005], [S004], [S032], [S039], [S041], [S042] |
| EL-028 | Multi-tier dependency visibility and resilience segmentation | Maintain decision-useful deep-tier visibility only for critical dependencies, coupled to buffer, alternate and recovery action. | Huge stale maps; supplier confidentiality conflict; false sense of control; concentration persists despite visibility; mapping cost exceeds value. | HIGH_POST_DISRUPTION_DOMAIN | [S035], [S036], [S039], [S040], [S037], [S040] |
| EL-031 | Software delivery flow with automation, observability, reliability and recovery | Optimise sustainable software change across throughput and instability, with small reversible changes, outcome validation and humane operations. | Pipeline theatre; flaky tests; metric gaming; developer burnout; rapid delivery of low-value work; fragile platforms; operations burden hidden. | MODERATE_LARGE_OBSERVATIONAL | [S053], [S054], [S057], [S055], [S056], [S059] |

## EVOLVED_LEAN_CONTEXT_SPECIFIC_PROPERTIES

| PROPERTY_ID | PROPERTY_NAME | CURRENT_STATUS | TRIGGER | NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH | DEPENDENCIES | OPEN_QUESTION |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EL-003 | Workplace and work-state legibility | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Work is repeatedly lost, searched for, misrouted, duplicated or left in an ambiguous state. | When state is already obvious and low-risk, use naming conventions or a small status field; do not create ritual boards or audit scoring. | Shared state definitions; maintenance ownership; accessibility; protection against surveillance and stale displays. | How much legibility improves outcomes independently of accompanying management attention remains weakly identified. |
| EL-004 | Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Arrival pressure exceeds completion capacity, active work accumulates, or long WIP correlates with delay/quality loss. | For low-volume work with naturally bounded commitments, use a simple finish-before-start norm or periodic capacity check. | Observable WIP boundary; stable item definitions; capacity/replenishment capability; priority and exception policy; no hidden pre-entry queue. | What causal evidence is sufficient to distinguish a useful limit from demand suppression or work reclassification? |
| EL-005 | Batch-size economics and small reversible increments | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Feedback delay or failure blast radius is material and switching/release cost can be reduced. | If changes are already tiny or validation has a fixed high cost, keep the economic batch and improve feedback within it. | Measurable setup/release costs; modular work; validation capability; rollback/recovery; ability to integrate increments. | How should economic batch size be estimated where quality evidence is sparse or validation cost is nonlinear? |
| EL-007 | Demand-capacity pacing and production levelling | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Repeatable work, measurable aggregate demand, flexible capacity and feasible mix sequencing. | For unique, exploratory or very low-volume work, use finite-capacity planning, rolling forecasts and explicit priority rather than takt ceremony. | Demand information; cross-trained/flexible resources; setup capability; exception policy; workload and ergonomic safeguards; buffers. | Robust rules for levelling highly variable knowledge work remain unresolved; evidence is dominated by manufacturing cases. |
| EL-010 | Demand and replenishment information integrity | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Replenishment or release decisions rely on demand signals across time or organisational boundaries. | For direct low-volume requests, use explicit confirmation and a simple backlog. | Trusted item identity; data quality; latency bounds; incentives that do not reward signal manipulation; long-lead visibility. | How should pull incorporate sparse catastrophic demand and strategic long-lead preparation? |
| EL-012 | Bounded stop, containment, escalation and recovery authority | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Expected propagation harm exceeds interruption and recovery cost, or safety/quality boundaries are breached. | For low-consequence reversible anomalies, flag and route to a bounded follow-up queue without stopping the whole system. | Psychological/employment safety; available responders; isolation capability; restart authority; false-stop review; recovery path. | Direct comparative evidence on stop-authority effectiveness under real production pressure and false alarms remains limited. |
| EL-014 | Mistake-proofing and fail-safe constraints | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Error mode is recurrent, well understood and cheaply preventable or detectable. | Use a clear cue or checklist for infrequent low-consequence tasks rather than hard constraint. | Accurate error model; escape/override for legitimate exceptions; human-factors testing; maintenance. | Evidence comparing hard prevention, detection and training across complex socio-technical errors is incomplete. |
| EL-015 | Jidoka/autonomation: automate routine operation while reserving human exception judgement | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Routine is stable enough to automate, abnormalities are detectable, and human attention is scarce or high-value. | Keep manual work where volume is low, judgement dominates or automation cannot fail safely. | Reliable detection; safe state; human response capacity; understandable handoff; maintenance; workload monitoring. | How should organisations preserve human competence when automation makes intervention rare but consequential? |
| EL-017 | Explicit current baseline with controlled revision | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Work recurs, consistency matters, multiple people perform it or failure consequences require a known baseline. | For one-off low-risk work, retain a short decision record or checklist rather than a full standard-work sheet. | Worker input; valid current evidence; change and exception authority; review owner/date; ergonomic and safety assessment. | Historical evidence for explicit expiry/retirement inside Toyota is limited; those safeguards are evolved engineering additions. |
| EL-018 | Direct observation combined with traceable system evidence | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Material uncertainty exists about what actually happens or reports conflict with outcomes. | For well-instrumented, low-risk routine work, sample direct observation periodically and investigate exceptions. | Access; non-punitive observation; representative sampling; traceability; skill in distinguishing observation from inference. | How much observation frequency is enough under rapid change, privacy constraints and distributed work? |
| EL-020 | Improvement sustainment, expiry, retirement and economics | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | A countermeasure changes recurring work or introduces continuing control/maintenance cost. | For ephemeral low-risk changes, define a short observation period and automatic expiry. | Baseline and outcome data; ownership; ability to remove controls; preserved rationale; leadership continuity. | Long-term comparative evidence on retirement and deimplementation inside Lean programmes is limited. |
| EL-021 | Worker capability, training and cross-skilling | SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT_PROPERTY | Work recurs, failures require local response, or capacity flexibility depends on transferable skill. | For rare low-risk work, retain accessible guidance and an expert escalation path rather than broad certification. | Paid training time; qualified instructors; humane workload; current baselines; opportunities to practise; recognition of expertise. | Evidence separating training benefit from selection, staffing and management quality is incomplete. |
| EL-022 | Safe voice, participation and bounded change authority | SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT_PROPERTY | Front-line observation is necessary for quality, safety or improvement and management cannot centrally see conditions. | For simple low-risk work, a direct owner contact and documented response is enough. | Employment/psychological safety; response obligation; workload and time; representation; transparent decisions; escalation above local management. | Which governance arrangements reliably preserve voice under cost and schedule pressure across labour regimes? |
| EL-023 | Employment security, trust and fair distribution of productivity gains | SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT_PROPERTY | Improvement solicits worker knowledge, reduces labour content or changes job design materially. | For small changes with no employment/workload effect, state that boundary and monitor cumulative effects. | Credible management commitment; financial capacity; worker representation/voice; redeployment and training; transparent gain accounting. | No universal employment-security design exists for volatile firms, contractors or supply networks; this remains institution-dependent. |
| EL-025 | Whole-system optimisation and proxy/metric governance | USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED | Metrics affect incentives or decisions across interacting components. | For a small team with direct outcome visibility, use a few narrative/quantitative checks rather than a dashboard programme. | Outcome model; stable metric definitions; cross-boundary data; qualitative challenge; no punishment for adverse signals. | How can metric gaming be detected before it materially distorts the work? |
| EL-027 | Supplier and dependency capability development with fair risk allocation | SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT_PROPERTY | A dependency is recurrent, strategically material and improvable through relationship-specific learning. | For commodity, low-risk purchases, use clear specifications, diversified sources and routine qualification rather than a development programme. | Long-term relationship; technical openness; bargaining fairness; supplier capacity; protection of proprietary knowledge; exit alternatives. | Independent evidence on how Toyota-style supplier development distributes gains, workload and risk across tiers remains incomplete. |
| EL-029 | Set-based option preservation and delayed irreversible convergence | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Uncertainty, coupling and irreversibility are high enough that a wrong early choice is costly and parallel learning is affordable. | When the decision is reversible, familiar or low-coupling, choose one option and preserve a fallback note. | Capacity for parallel analysis; explicit decision criteria; integration/trade-off knowledge; convergence deadlines; ownership. | Strong comparative causal evidence outside automotive/product design remains limited. |
| EL-030 | Cross-functional product-development integration and reusable trade-off knowledge | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Product outcomes couple multiple disciplines, lifecycle stages or downstream operations. | For a small single-discipline change, direct peer review and a concise decision record suffice. | Representative disciplines; decision authority; shared models; knowledge stewardship; incentives for lifecycle rather than functional optimisation. | Which integration structures work best outside automotive and how reusable knowledge remains valid under technology change are unresolved. |
| EL-031 | Software delivery flow with automation, observability, reliability and recovery | RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM | Software changes are frequent enough that manual release and delayed integration impose risk or delay. | For infrequent low-risk software, use reproducible builds, basic automated tests and a documented release/recovery path. | Version control; trustworthy tests; environment/configuration control; observability; on-call/recovery capability; product outcome feedback. | Causal effect sizes, optimal limits and metric robustness across software domains remain unresolved. |
| EL-032 | Portfolio selection, stopping and retirement of low-value work | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT | Demand exceeds capacity, work ages without evidence, or a value hypothesis fails. | For a tiny backlog, review informally at a defined interval and close stale items. | Outcome evidence; total-cost visibility; authority to stop; reversible archival/transition; stakeholder communication. | How should long-horizon public value and option value be preserved when near-term evidence is weak? |

### Manufacturing-specific forms and limits

| PROPERTY_OR_FORM | PROPERTY_IDS | LIMIT | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Classic takt time and heijunka sequence | EL-007 | Requires recurring demand units, measurable work content and controllable mix; use workload/capacity approaches elsewhere. | [S004], [S063], [S064] |
| Physical kanban containers/cards between repeat processes | EL-004, EL-010 | The general property is WIP/replenishment control; physical signalling is domain-specific. | [S003], [S006], [S032] |
| One-piece moving assembly flow and manufacturing cells | EL-005, EL-006 | Generalises only as batch/transfer economics and feedback; not every service or design path is linear. | [S004], [S032] |
| Fixed-position assembly-line stop with small line buffers | EL-012 | Generalises to bounded containment/escalation; exact geometry/timing is manufacturing-specific. | [S004], [S005] |
| Standard WIP as part of standardised assembly work | EL-017 | Generalises to explicit baseline; classic takt/sequence/standard-WIP form does not fit all work. | [S004] |
| SMED stamping-die changeover practice | EL-006 | Generalises to setup/release latency reduction, not the specific industrial technique. | [S004], [S007] |
| Supplier milk-runs and tightly timed automotive replenishment | EL-010, EL-027, EL-028 | Depends on geography, lead time, volume, transport and risk; no universal supply policy. | [S005], [S040], [S069] |
| Machine autonomation from textile/automotive equipment | EL-015 | Generalises to bounded automation and human exception handling; detector/stop implementation is technology-specific. | [S001], [S067] |
| Physical 5S/shadow-board/floor-layout controls | EL-003 | Generalises only where physical search, missing tools or ergonomic state are material. | [S066] |
| Automotive set-based concurrent engineering implementation | EL-029, EL-030 | The option and trade-off properties may transfer; exact organisation and artefacts are domain-specific. | [S049], [S050], [S052] |

## EVOLVED_LEAN_REJECTED_OR_SUPERSEDED_PRACTICES

| PROPERTY_ID | PROPOSITION | CURRENT_STATUS | WHY_REJECTED_OR_SUPERSEDED | MATURE_REPLACEMENT | DISPOSITION_NOTE | EVIDENCE_STRENGTH |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EL-033 | Universal waste elimination as the master objective | SUPERSEDED_BY_STRONGER_FORM | Waste is easier to name than value; universal elimination ignores variability, option value and externalities. | No independent property. Ask which failure mode the alleged waste causes and what protective function it may serve. | Rejected as an independent crosswalk property; retained only as a prompt subordinate to stronger properties. | HIGH_CRITICAL_CONVERGENT |
