| EL-034 | Minimum or zero inventory everywhere | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | Toyota sources specify minimum necessary stock, not zero; empirical performance is often concave and disruption research rejects binary JIT/JIC. | NO GENERAL PROPERTY; choose inventory by total expected cost and resilience. | Rejected universal rule; component-specific inventory optimisation remains valid. | HIGH_CONTRARY_EVIDENCE |
| EL-035 | Maximum utilisation and zero slack | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | Operations science shows capacity is one of the required variability buffers; labour evidence links intensification to harm. | Target sustainable service and total cost, not maximum busy time. | Rejected universal rule. | HIGH_CONTRARY_EVIDENCE |
| EL-036 | One-piece flow or the smallest possible batch everywhere | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | Knowledge/product development may benefit from coherent experiments or parallel option sets; HMLV settings require adaptation. | NO GENERAL PROPERTY; choose reversible economic batches by context. | Rejected universal rule; local one-piece flow can still be optimal. | HIGH_MECHANISTIC_CONTEXTUAL |
| EL-037 | Exact Toyota-tool or named-method conformance | CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY | Empirical implementation research finds soft/system conditions distinguish outcomes more than possession of hard tools. | NO GENERAL PROPERTY. Evaluate mechanism, trigger, burden, alternatives and outcome. | Rejected as a property; all named practices are optional implementations. | HIGH_CONVERGENT |
| EL-038 | Improvement activity volume as evidence of improvement | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | Kaizen/PDSA studies show method fidelity and sustainment vary; completed events do not establish customer/system payoff. | Count evidence-qualified outcomes and retired failures, not ritual volume. | Rejected as outcome evidence. | HIGH_CRITICAL |
| EL-039 | Universal supplier synchronisation, concentration or single sourcing | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | Post-2011 evidence and JIT risk studies show synchronisation/centralisation can raise disruption exposure. | Choose relationship depth and source redundancy by criticality, learning value and recovery economics. | Rejected universal rule; selective integration remains valid. | HIGH_POST_DISRUPTION |
| EL-040 | Immutable standardisation and procedure compliance | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | Worker studies link workflow formalisation and autonomy loss to poorer outcomes; mature Lean interpretations describe standards as improvable baselines. | No immutable general standard; use risk-calibrated constraints within a versioned and reviewable baseline. | Rejected universal rule; risk-based mandatory constraints remain possible within EL-017. | HIGH_MIXED_SOCIAL |

| OQ-01 | How should constrained value aggregate customer, worker, supplier, public-safety and ecological interests when they conflict? | EL-001, EL-023, EL-027 | No Lean-native aggregation rule resolves distributive conflict. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-02 | What empirical threshold identifies when additional end-to-end mapping is worth its instrumentation and maintenance cost? | EL-002, EL-003 | Most evidence is case-based and bundled with management attention. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-03 | How should WIP limits be tuned for heterogeneous work classes, blocked work and rare urgent demand without creating hidden queues? | EL-004, EL-009 | Formal queue models simplify priority, dependency and behavioural gaming. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-04 | What decision rule best balances batch size, setup cost, validation cost, option value and failure blast radius in knowledge work? | EL-005, EL-006, EL-029 | No universal economic model covers all software/design contexts. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-05 | Where is classic takt/levelling superior to workload control or dynamic scheduling in high-mix, high-variability environments? | EL-007 | Evidence is domain-specific and implementation-dependent. | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT |
| OQ-06 | How should organisations quantify low-frequency disruption benefits so strategic buffers survive short-horizon cost pressure? | EL-008, EL-028 | Tail probabilities, common-mode dependencies and option viability are uncertain. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-07 | How much capacity slack is economically optimal when queueing, maintenance, learning and recovery share the same reserve? | EL-008, EL-009, EL-026 | Costs and uses interact; labels can hide unowned idle time. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-08 | How can pull systems preserve signal integrity under cancellations, strategic demand shaping and automated feedback loops? | EL-004, EL-010 | Historical kanban assumes stronger physical coupling than many digital markets provide. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-09 | What alarm/stop thresholds minimise combined false-negative propagation and false-positive interruption cost in different consequence classes? | EL-011, EL-012 | Thresholds are technology- and hazard-specific; Lean literature is not sufficient. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-10 | When does mistake-proofing reduce adaptive capacity or create automation complacency? | EL-014, EL-015 | Benefits are strong for stable error modes but side effects are context-specific. | CONTEXT_DEPENDENT |
| OQ-11 | What minimum statistical capability should precede aggressive inventory/WIP reduction? | EL-004, EL-008, EL-016, EL-026 | No single threshold covers service consequence, lead time and failure distribution. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-12 | How should standard-work expiry and retirement be calibrated without causing needless review bureaucracy? | EL-017, EL-020 | Expiry/retirement are evolved safeguards with limited direct comparative evidence. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-13 | Which problem types can safely use a lightweight Five-Whys prompt, and when is systems causal analysis mandatory? | EL-019 | Complexity/consequence boundaries require domain safety knowledge. | UNRESOLVED |
| OQ-14 | What designs make frontline participation genuinely decision-bearing rather than an intensification mechanism? | EL-021, EL-022, EL-023 | Empirical effects remain mixed across work complexity, labour institutions and management choices. | CONTESTED |
| OQ-15 | How durable are employment-security and gain-sharing commitments under downturns, automation and contingent work? | EL-023 | Historical compacts do not generalise across legal and economic regimes. | UNRESOLVED |

| OQ-18 | How should supplier-development gains and resilience costs be allocated so the focal firm does not externalise risk? | EL-027, EL-028 | Toyota network evidence shows capability benefits but not a universal fair-allocation model. | UNRESOLVED |
