## EVOLVED_LEAN_AUDIT_INTAKE

```text
PROPERTY_POPULATION_TOTAL: 40
PROPERTY_POPULATION_EXAMINED: 40
PROPERTY_COVERAGE: 40/40
CROSSWALK_WORTHY_PROPERTY_TOTAL: 32
REJECTED_SUPERSEDED_OR_CEREMONY_TOTAL: 8
```

### TOP_CROSSWALK_PROPERTIES

All `EL-001`–`EL-032` properties are included below because “crosswalk-worthy” does not mean “always adopt.” It means the separate repository audit should test whether the native system already closes the failure mode, whether the trigger exists, whether preconditions hold, and whether adoption would add mechanism rather than terminology.


#### EL-001 — Outcome and value definition with named beneficiary, constraints and time horizon

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-001
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Outcome and value definition with named beneficiary, constraints and time horizon
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Efficient delivery of low-value, harmful or locally beneficial work. Known over-application or implementation failures: Customer rhetoric masks managerial cost goals; one stakeholder's value harms another; short-horizon metrics; unmeasurable outcomes; retrospective rationalisation.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Use an explicit value hypothesis with beneficiary, outcome, constraints, evidence threshold, review date and abandonment rule.
- **TRIGGER:** Multiple plausible outputs compete, resources are material, or local efficiency could displace customer/system value.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For trivial, reversible work, record a one-sentence purpose and acceptance condition rather than create a value-stream artefact.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Legitimate beneficiary representation; outcome observability; explicit non-negotiable constraints; authority to stop low-value work.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_CONVERGENT; primary [S010], [S011], [S018]; critical [S024], [S060], [S065]; domain/empirical [S062], [S060], [S061]
- **CRITICISMS:** Lean literature specifies waste more richly than value; customer language can ignore workers, suppliers, public risk and long-term resilience.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** No Lean vocabulary, value workshop or value-stream map is required; the property is a decision-relevant outcome model.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-009, EL-023, EL-008
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the target system name the beneficiary, desired outcome, constraints and time horizon rather than equating output or waste removal with value?
  - Can it stop or retire work when the value hypothesis fails?
  - Are labour, safety, resilience and external costs prevented from disappearing behind a customer-value claim?

#### EL-002 — End-to-end system boundary and flow/queue/handoff visibility

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-002
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** End-to-end system boundary and flow/queue/handoff visibility
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Local optimisation and invisible waiting across organisational boundaries. Known over-application or implementation failures: Map theatre; stale diagrams; excessive instrumentation; forced linear path for exploratory work; omission of externalities; local teams unable to change system constraints.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Maintain the cheapest decision-useful end-to-end model, including feedback/rework loops, queues, dependencies and outcome—not just touch time.
- **TRIGGER:** Repeated handoffs, unexplained elapsed time, departmental optimisation or customer delay dominate processing time.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a short single-owner path, a simple trace or sampled timeline is enough; no wall-sized map.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** End-to-end latency, queue and handoff profile; assumes valid item identity and boundary. Over-instrumentation and forced linearisation are failure modes.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable item identity; traceability across boundaries; truthful timestamps/states; an owner able to resolve cross-boundary causes.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT; primary [S003], [S005], [S018]; critical [S060], [S065]; domain/empirical [S020], [S061], [S058]
- **CRITICISMS:** Value-stream maps are frequently local snapshots and can mistake visible factory-like sequence for the real distributed causal system.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** A value-stream map is one implementation. Event logs, service blueprints, dependency graphs or sampled traces may be stronger.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-018, EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system expose end-to-end waiting, queues, handoffs, rework and outcome latency rather than only local activity?
  - Is the map or trace current enough to change a decision?
  - Does it preserve a cheap path where the work is already short, observable and single-owner?

#### EL-003 — Workplace and work-state legibility

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-003
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Workplace and work-state legibility
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Undetected abnormality, lost work, excessive search and ambiguous ownership. Known over-application or implementation failures: 5S audit theatre; cosmetic tidiness; information overload; stale boards; worker surveillance; local legibility hiding end-to-end state.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Make the minimum relevant normal/abnormal state visible to people who can act, with freshness, accessibility and ownership controls.
- **TRIGGER:** Work is repeatedly lost, searched for, misrouted, duplicated or left in an ambiguous state.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** When state is already obvious and low-risk, use naming conventions or a small status field; do not create ritual boards or audit scoring.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Shared state definitions; maintenance ownership; accessibility; protection against surveillance and stale displays.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_EMPIRICAL; primary [S001], [S004], [S005]; critical [S025], [S026], [S066]; domain/empirical [S066], [S055], [S061]
- **CRITICISMS:** Evidence for exact 5S bundles is small and heterogeneous; legibility can become discipline and scoring rather than aid.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** 5S labels, shadow boards and kanban columns are optional; cleanliness scores are not the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-023, EL-025
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Can responsible people tell the current and abnormal state without reconstructing it manually?
  - Are displays fresh, action-linked and non-punitive rather than ceremonial surveillance?
  - Would additional visual management solve a real ambiguity or merely add another board?

#### EL-004 — Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-004
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Queue growth, overproduction, premature commitment and excessive context switching. Known over-application or implementation failures: Starvation; hidden backlog before entry; arbitrary limits; gaming item size/status; blocking critical long-lead work; apparent cycle-time gains through exclusion.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Use empirical limits by work class, monitor blocked/aged work and total demand, and adapt limits with capacity, variability and service risk.
- **TRIGGER:** Arrival pressure exceeds completion capacity, active work accumulates, or long WIP correlates with delay/quality loss.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For low-volume work with naturally bounded commitments, use a simple finish-before-start norm or periodic capacity check.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: congestion and feedback reduction. Assumes a valid boundary and enough response capacity. Over-application causes starvation or displaced queues. Pair with EL-008 and EL-010.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Observable WIP boundary; stable item definitions; capacity/replenishment capability; priority and exception policy; no hidden pre-entry queue.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL; primary [S003], [S005], [S006]; critical [S032], [S033], [S039]; domain/empirical [S032], [S034], [S055], [S056]
- **CRITICISMS:** A pull signal is not trustworthy demand or capacity; board-level limits can displace queues and invite metric gaming.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Kanban cards/boards are not required. A database rule, token, CONWIP, admission policy or finite-capacity scheduler can implement it.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-007, EL-008, EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the target system explicitly limit active commitments or merely display them?
  - Are hidden upstream queues, work-item splitting and exception classes visible?
  - Does the limit trigger only where congestion is a demonstrated failure mode?

#### EL-005 — Batch-size economics and small reversible increments

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-005
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Batch-size economics and small reversible increments
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Late defect discovery, large release risk, long queue time and irreversible commitment. Known over-application or implementation failures: One-piece-flow dogma; fragmentation overhead; excessive coordination; local micro-batches accumulating into system batches; unsafe partial delivery.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Select batch size from total economics and reversibility, not a universal minimum; make delayed feedback and blast radius explicit.
- **TRIGGER:** Feedback delay or failure blast radius is material and switching/release cost can be reduced.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** If changes are already tiny or validation has a fixed high cost, keep the economic batch and improve feedback within it.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: shorter feedback and smaller failure scope. Assumes manageable setup/validation. Overuse creates fragmentation and coordination waste.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurable setup/release costs; modular work; validation capability; rollback/recovery; ability to integrate increments.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL; primary [S004], [S007]; critical [S032], [S036]; domain/empirical [S057], [S061], [S062]
- **CRITICISMS:** Smallness is not value; a minimum batch can be uneconomic, and knowledge work may require coherent experimental units.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** One-piece flow, daily releases or story slicing are implementations, not the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-006, EL-029, EL-036
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system choose batch size from feedback, setup, validation and failure economics?
  - Can it use a cheap path when smaller batches would add ceremony or integration cost?
  - Are locally small batches still bundled into a large end-to-end release?

#### EL-006 — Setup, changeover, release and feedback latency reduction

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-006
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Setup, changeover, release and feedback latency reduction
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Long feedback loops, large economic batches, downtime and costly recovery. Known over-application or implementation failures: Unsafe shortcuts; preparation shifted to another team; brittle automation; ergonomic or quality risk; faster release of low-value work.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Reduce total safe transition cost, including preparation, validation, recovery and burden transferred across boundaries.
- **TRIGGER:** Setup, integration, release or environment preparation materially determines batch size, delay or downtime.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** If setup is rare and immaterial, document the path and avoid an optimisation programme.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: enables smaller batches and flexible capacity. Assumes quality and safety are preserved. Overuse hides transferred work or brittle automation.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Stable interfaces; safe automation; competent maintainers; configuration control; rollback and qualification.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT; primary [S004], [S007]; critical [S024], [S025]; domain/empirical [S032], [S057]
- **CRITICISMS:** Local setup savings often omit preparation, validation and recovery transferred elsewhere.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** SMED workshops, coloured steps and checklists are optional; lower total transition cost is the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-013, EL-023
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is batch size large because setup, release or feedback is expensive, and has that cost been attacked directly?
  - Does the metric include validation, recovery and transferred work?
  - Would automation lower total safe transition cost or merely automate a fragile process?

#### EL-007 — Demand-capacity pacing and production levelling

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-007
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Demand-capacity pacing and production levelling
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Demand/capacity mismatch, violent mix changes, uneven workloads and hidden overburden. Known over-application or implementation failures: False precision; backlog or overtime used to hide variation; forced pace and work intensification; brittle high-mix schedules; supplier burden transfer.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Level only forecastable aggregate demand, maintain capacity/time buffers and protect workload limits; treat takt as diagnostic, not a metronome.
- **TRIGGER:** Repeatable work, measurable aggregate demand, flexible capacity and feasible mix sequencing.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For unique, exploratory or very low-volume work, use finite-capacity planning, rolling forecasts and explicit priority rather than takt ceremony.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: reduces oscillation and overload. Assumes repeatability and demand aggregation. Overuse converts variability into backlog, overtime or supplier stress.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Demand information; cross-trained/flexible resources; setup capability; exception policy; workload and ergonomic safeguards; buffers.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_CONTEXTUAL; primary [S002], [S004], [S005]; critical [S025], [S026], [S064]; domain/empirical [S034], [S063], [S064]
- **CRITICISMS:** Takt can become a coercive pace standard; conventional levelling has weak applicability in high-mix/low-volume or discovery work.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Takt boards, heijunka boxes and fixed pitch are manufacturing implementations, not general requirements.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-008, EL-023, EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is the work repetitive and demand sufficiently observable for a pace or levelling rule to be meaningful?
  - Where does residual variation go—capacity reserve, inventory, delay, overtime or suppliers?
  - Does the mechanism reveal overload or impose an unsafe pace?

#### EL-008 — Explicit buffer portfolio and strategic slack

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-008
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Explicit buffer portfolio and strategic slack
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Fragility, starvation, burnout, missed service, recovery delay and hidden risk transfer. Known over-application or implementation failures: Slack becomes unmanaged delay; reserves are raided for utilisation; inventory obsolescence; redundancy hides defects; false quantitative precision.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Optimise a portfolio of justified buffers; distinguish protective reserve from accidental queue and continuously reassess assumptions.
- **TRIGGER:** Variability or disruption cannot be eliminated economically and service or failure consequences are material.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For low-risk, rapidly recoverable work, keep only a small contingency and explicit recovery path.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Core resilience property. Value: absorbs unavoidable variability. Overuse creates cost/latency; underuse creates fragility.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Demand/failure data; risk appetite; cross-boundary cost visibility; authority to hold reserve; anti-hoarding review.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT; primary [S002], [S006]; critical [S033], [S039]; domain/empirical [S032], [S034], [S040]
- **CRITICISMS:** Classic Lean rhetoric often treats inventory and slack as waste, while disruption and inventory-performance evidence shows non-monotonic returns.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Safety stock, spare capacity, backup suppliers or time contingencies are implementations. No universal buffer is mandatory.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-004, EL-007, EL-009, EL-034, EL-035
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system explicitly choose among inventory, capacity, time, information and redundancy buffers?
  - Can it distinguish strategic slack from accidental queueing?
  - Would proposed WIP or inventory reduction merely transfer risk or workload elsewhere?

#### EL-009 — Flow and queue-aware capacity over maximum local utilisation

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-009
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Flow and queue-aware capacity over maximum local utilisation
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Congestion, priority churn, long lead time and local output that cannot be absorbed downstream. Known over-application or implementation failures: Underutilisation without purpose; politically contested spare capacity; local teams penalised for system choices; bottleneck migration.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Optimise end-to-end service and total buffering cost; treat utilisation as a constraint or signal, not a target to maximise.
- **TRIGGER:** Queues grow despite high activity or local productivity gains fail to improve total outcome.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For low variability and ample capacity, simple utilisation monitoring plus exception handling is enough.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: avoids congestion collapse. Assumes a meaningful service boundary. Over-application leaves costly idle capacity; under-application creates nonlinear queues.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Valid system boundary; capacity/arrival data; authority across silos; protected slack; outcome measures.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT; primary [S003], [S005]; critical [S024], [S025]; domain/empirical [S032], [S056], [S062]
- **CRITICISMS:** Lean deployments may still pursue labour or machine utilisation and headcount reduction despite flow doctrine.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Cell layouts or line-balancing charts are not required; queue-aware admission and capacity rules are alternatives.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-008, EL-035
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the target system optimise end-to-end throughput and latency or reward local busy time and output?
  - Is reserve capacity protected where variability makes high utilisation dangerous?
  - Are queue costs and the location of the constraint visible?

#### EL-010 — Demand and replenishment information integrity

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-010
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Demand and replenishment information integrity
- **FAILURE_MODE:** False demand, bullwhip, duplicate work, priority gaming and long-lead omissions. Known over-application or implementation failures: Demand is endogenous or manipulated; urgent work bypasses signals; forecasts ignored; local pull cannot prepare long-lead components.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A governed demand-information contract, not blind reaction to the latest request.
- **TRIGGER:** Replenishment or release decisions rely on demand signals across time or organisational boundaries.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For direct low-volume requests, use explicit confirmation and a simple backlog.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: truthful release and replenishment. Assumes timely signals and response capability. Failure creates starvation, oscillation or false urgency.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Trusted item identity; data quality; latency bounds; incentives that do not reward signal manipulation; long-lead visibility.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL; primary [S002], [S003], [S005]; critical [S039], [S040]; domain/empirical [S032], [S034], [S035]
- **CRITICISMS:** A visible pull signal does not establish trustworthy demand or adequate replenishment capability.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Physical cards, supermarkets and board movements are optional implementations.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-007, EL-008
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is the demand or pull signal timely, semantically clear and resistant to gaming?
  - Does the system complement consumption with long-lead, capacity and risk information?
  - Can urgent exceptions be distinguished from habitual bypass?

#### EL-011 — Abnormality detection and signalling

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-011
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Abnormality detection and signalling
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Silent propagation, delayed response and normalisation of deviation. Known over-application or implementation failures: False alarms, missed detections, alarm fatigue, ambiguous ownership, suppressed signals and throughput pressure.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Detect and signal decision-relevant abnormalities with calibrated severity, accountable response and feedback on false positives/negatives.
- **TRIGGER:** Continued operation after an abnormality materially increases harm, rework, delay or uncertainty.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For rare, low-consequence events, use a manual report with clear ownership rather than continuous instrumentation.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Known normal envelope; usable detectors; low-friction human signalling; response ownership; alarm review.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT; primary [S001], [S004], [S005], [S067]; critical [S025], [S029]; domain/empirical [S031], [S067]
- **CRITICISMS:** Detection without response creates theatre; automated detection can encode an incomplete model and workers may be punished for signalling.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** An andon cord or light is optional; alerts, checks, guardrails or human escalation can implement the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-012, EL-023, EL-025
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system expose material abnormalities early enough to change the outcome?
  - Are detection accuracy, alarm burden and ownership monitored?
  - Can a person signal safely when automated detection is absent or wrong?

#### EL-012 — Bounded stop, containment, escalation and recovery authority

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-012
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Bounded stop, containment, escalation and recovery authority
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Defect propagation, unsafe continuation and unmanaged emergency escalation. Known over-application or implementation failures: False stops, alarm fatigue, punitive culture, ignored calls, local stop causing larger system harm, rapid reset without diagnosis.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Severity-calibrated, non-punitive stop/containment authority with response time, restart criteria, evidence preservation and post-event learning.
- **TRIGGER:** Expected propagation harm exceeds interruption and recovery cost, or safety/quality boundaries are breached.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For low-consequence reversible anomalies, flag and route to a bounded follow-up queue without stopping the whole system.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Trade-off: containment reduces propagation but interrupts flow. Trigger by severity and expected loss; cheap path is flag-and-follow-up for low consequence.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Psychological/employment safety; available responders; isolation capability; restart authority; false-stop review; recovery path.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_HISTORICAL_MECHANISTIC; primary [S004], [S005], [S006], [S067]; critical [S025], [S029]; domain/empirical [S031]
- **CRITICISMS:** A cord does not confer real authority; line pressure can suppress use, and indiscriminate stopping is costly.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** A physical cord, red light or universal stop is not required; bounded isolation and escalation are the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-009, EL-023, EL-026
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system give abnormalities a bounded stop, containment or escalation path rather than merely record them?
  - Who may stop, who must respond, and who may restart?
  - Does throughput pressure or punishment make the nominal authority unusable?

#### EL-013 — Quality at source and defect containment

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-013
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Quality at source and defect containment
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Downstream rework, defect multiplication, ambiguous ownership and late customer harm. Known over-application or implementation failures: Over-testing; brittle checks; test gaming; false confidence; quality burden shifted to operators; local pass misses system failure.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Use risk-based source verification and containment with independent system-level assurance where local checks cannot see emergent failure.
- **TRIGGER:** Defects become more costly or less diagnosable downstream, and source checks have useful sensitivity.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For very low-risk reversible output, use sampled or downstream validation with traceability.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Testability; clear quality criteria; containment path; no incentive to hide defects; qualified repair/release.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT; primary [S001], [S005], [S010]; critical [S046], [S060]; domain/empirical [S041], [S057], [S061]
- **CRITICISMS:** Quality at source can become blame at source; inspection does not equal process capability or causal prevention.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Inspection stamps, quality gates or test suites are implementations; early trustworthy containment is the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-005, EL-006, EL-009
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Are defects detected and contained before avoidable downstream propagation?
  - Does a local pass actually establish system quality, or is independent assurance needed?
  - Can workers report defects without absorbing blame for structural causes?

#### EL-014 — Mistake-proofing and fail-safe constraints

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-014
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Mistake-proofing and fail-safe constraints
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Repeatable human error, wrong-part use, omitted steps and unsafe state transitions. Known over-application or implementation failures: Brittle constraints; workarounds; blocking legitimate variation; new failure modes; ignored sensor faults; operator deskilling.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Risk-based constraint or detection for stable error modes, with tested override, monitoring and revision.
- **TRIGGER:** Error mode is recurrent, well understood and cheaply preventable or detectable.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Use a clear cue or checklist for infrequent low-consequence tasks rather than hard constraint.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Accurate error model; escape/override for legitimate exceptions; human-factors testing; maintenance.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL; primary [S004], [S005], [S067]; critical [S045], [S046]; domain/empirical [S041], [S057]
- **CRITICISMS:** A device can freeze an incorrect process model and shift error elsewhere; not all complex failure has a local mistake.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** A branded poka-yoke device is unnecessary; any verified fail-safe design may implement the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-017, EL-022, EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is there a recurrent, well-understood error mode that design can remove or expose?
  - Does the constraint preserve legitimate exceptions and fail safely?
  - Would a hard control solve the cause or merely force workarounds?

#### EL-015 — Jidoka/autonomation: automate routine operation while reserving human exception judgement

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-015
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Jidoka/autonomation: automate routine operation while reserving human exception judgement
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Unattended defect propagation and wasteful human machine-watching. Known over-application or implementation failures: Automation bias; deskilling; opaque models; alert overload; unsafe autonomous continuation; humans left with only rare hard cases.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Automate only stable, testable routine work; make uncertainty and abnormality explicit; preserve human competence and safe fallback.
- **TRIGGER:** Routine is stable enough to automate, abnormalities are detectable, and human attention is scarce or high-value.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Keep manual work where volume is low, judgement dominates or automation cannot fail safely.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Reliable detection; safe state; human response capacity; understandable handoff; maintenance; workload monitoring.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_HISTORICAL_MODERATE_TRANSFER; primary [S001], [S005], [S067]; critical [S025], [S026], [S029]; domain/empirical [S041], [S057]
- **CRITICISMS:** Automation may be justified as labour reduction and can worsen surveillance, pace or job quality; 'human touch' rhetoric is not sufficient.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Toyota-style machines, andon lights or robots are not required; bounded human-machine allocation is the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-021, EL-023, EL-026
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is automation limited to stable routine work with safe exception handoff?
  - Can humans understand, override and recover the system?
  - Does the automation reduce harmful work or merely reduce headcount and concentrate cognitive risk?

#### EL-016 — Process capability, variation measurement and statistical quality control

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-016
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Process capability, variation measurement and statistical quality control
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Tampering, recurrent defects, false improvement and unstable output. Known over-application or implementation failures: Chart ritual; bad data; arbitrary limits; metric gaming; treating statistical control as fitness for use; ignoring rare catastrophic modes.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Use fit-for-purpose variation analysis and capability evidence, explicitly separating stability, customer need and risk.
- **TRIGGER:** Repeated measurable output where variation matters and observations are sufficiently comparable.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For rare or unique work, use structured case evidence and direct tests rather than force a control chart.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Measurement-system validity; enough observations; statistical competence; stable definitions; authority to change systemic causes.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_HISTORICAL_EMPIRICAL; primary [S010], [S011]; critical [S045], [S047]; domain/empirical [S041], [S042], [S062]
- **CRITICISMS:** Lean accounts sometimes appropriate quality ancestry; Deming did not originate JIT or jidoka, and exact influence on A3/5 Whys is unproven.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** A control-chart poster, Deming Prize programme or Six Sigma belt is not required.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-018, EL-025
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the target distinguish signal from routine variation before changing the process?
  - Are measurements valid and linked to fitness for use rather than merely stable?
  - Would statistical machinery add value for this population or create false precision?

#### EL-017 — Explicit current baseline with controlled revision

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-017
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Explicit current baseline with controlled revision
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Uncontrolled variation, knowledge loss, untestable change and inconsistent training. Known over-application or implementation failures: Immutable procedure; stale paperwork; coercive pace; deskilling; suppressed expert judgement; standards written by distant staff; unauthorised workarounds.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A scoped, versioned, teachable baseline with exceptions, evidence, local challenge rights, qualification, expiry and retirement.
- **TRIGGER:** Work recurs, consistency matters, multiple people perform it or failure consequences require a known baseline.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For one-off low-risk work, retain a short decision record or checklist rather than a full standard-work sheet.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Worker input; valid current evidence; change and exception authority; review owner/date; ergonomic and safety assessment.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_HISTORICAL_MIXED_SOCIAL; primary [S004], [S008], [S009]; critical [S025], [S026], [S028]; domain/empirical [S020], [S031], [S041]
- **CRITICISMS:** Labour studies show formalisation can reduce autonomy and wellbeing; Toyota doctrine does not prove democratic control.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** A standard-work sheet is optional. Executable checks, runbooks, contracts or annotated examples can serve.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-014, EL-022, EL-029, EL-040
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system distinguish a current baseline from an immutable rule?
  - Who may deviate, propose revision, qualify the change and retire the standard?
  - Is the baseline still valid, usable and safe rather than paperwork compliance?

#### EL-018 — Direct observation combined with traceable system evidence

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-018
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Direct observation combined with traceable system evidence
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Decision by hearsay, dashboard-only management, executive theatre and local anecdotal causation. Known over-application or implementation failures: Gemba walks as theatre; surveillance; observer effects; anecdotal overreach; privileging visible local work over invisible dependencies.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Triangulate actual-work observation, system traces, outcome data and stakeholder accounts; label inference and uncertainty.
- **TRIGGER:** Material uncertainty exists about what actually happens or reports conflict with outcomes.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For well-instrumented, low-risk routine work, sample direct observation periodically and investigate exceptions.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Access; non-punitive observation; representative sampling; traceability; skill in distinguishing observation from inference.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_CONVERGENT; primary [S010], [S068]; critical [S029], [S060]; domain/empirical [S020], [S031], [S061]
- **CRITICISMS:** Direct seeing is neither sufficient nor neutral; distributed software, supply networks and latent harms require instrumentation and testimony.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** A scheduled gemba walk is not required; contextual inquiry, trace review or incident reconstruction may be better.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-016, EL-023, EL-025
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system obtain evidence from actual work rather than reports alone?
  - Is direct observation triangulated with traces, outcomes and affected people's testimony?
  - Could the observation process distort work or become surveillance?

#### EL-019 — Evidence-bound problem solving and countermeasure qualification

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-019
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Evidence-bound problem solving and countermeasure qualification
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Template-driven action, single-root stories, recurrence and false learning. Known over-application or implementation failures: A3/5-Whys ritual; confirmation bias; singular root cause; weak controls; no prediction; post hoc success; political exclusion of structural causes.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A proportional causal-learning loop with competing hypotheses, predictions, safe tests, outcome/side-effect evidence and recurrence review.
- **TRIGGER:** The failure is material, recurrent, causally uncertain or costly enough to justify investigation.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a trivial one-off reversible issue, fix and record the observation; escalate only on recurrence.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Time and skill; data/provenance; ability to test; independent challenge; authority over causes; follow-up owner.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT_WEAK_FORMAT_SPECIFIC; primary [S007], [S010], [S012]; critical [S045], [S046], [S048]; domain/empirical [S043], [S047]
- **CRITICISMS:** Format-specific causal evidence is weak; reviews show PDSA often used without core features and RCA often produces weak actions.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** A3 paper, exactly five whys, fishbone or PDCA labels are optional prompts and never evidence.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-020, EL-024, EL-038
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does repeated failure produce competing causal hypotheses and a qualified countermeasure rather than a completed template?
  - Are predictions, disconfirming evidence, side effects and recurrence checked?
  - Would a cheap fix suffice, or is the causal uncertainty consequential enough to trigger the full loop?

#### EL-020 — Improvement sustainment, expiry, retirement and economics

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-020
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Improvement sustainment, expiry, retirement and economics
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Improvement theatre, regression, zombie controls and unbounded process complexity. Known over-application or implementation failures: Audit-only sustainment; permanent controls after risk changes; sunk-cost defence; leadership turnover; benefits not remeasured.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Every material improvement is a governed hypothesis with benefit, burden, review, expiry and retirement path.
- **TRIGGER:** A countermeasure changes recurring work or introduces continuing control/maintenance cost.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For ephemeral low-risk changes, define a short observation period and automatic expiry.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Baseline and outcome data; ownership; ability to remove controls; preserved rationale; leadership continuity.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_EMPIRICAL; primary [S020]; critical [S046], [S048], [S060]; domain/empirical [S043], [S044], [S061]
- **CRITICISMS:** Kaizen-event studies show human outcomes and gains require follow-up; local improvements often lack customer or system outcome evidence.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Follow-up audits or control plans are implementations; evidence-bearing lifecycle ownership is the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-017, EL-038, EL-040
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Who owns the improvement after launch, and when is benefit rechecked?
  - Can obsolete standards, controls and metrics expire or be retired?
  - Does maintenance cost exceed the current value of the improvement?

#### EL-021 — Worker capability, training and cross-skilling

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-021
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Worker capability, training and cross-skilling
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Skill bottlenecks, unsafe improvisation, slow recovery and dependence on a few experts. Known over-application or implementation failures: Training as compliance; skill matrices without ability; unpaid improvement burden; multiskilling used only to cut staffing; obsolete instruction.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Maintain verified capability and cross-coverage for material work, paired with authority, workload protection and fair recognition.
- **TRIGGER:** Work recurs, failures require local response, or capacity flexibility depends on transferable skill.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For rare low-risk work, retain accessible guidance and an expert escalation path rather than broad certification.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Paid training time; qualified instructors; humane workload; current baselines; opportunities to practise; recognition of expertise.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_HISTORICAL_MIXED_EMPIRICAL; primary [S006], [S008], [S009]; critical [S025], [S026], [S028]; domain/empirical [S031], [S041], [S070]
- **CRITICISMS:** Participation and cross-skilling can intensify work and widen responsibility without control, pay or security.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** TWI cards, dojos and skill matrices are optional; demonstrated capability and coverage are the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-015, EL-023, EL-035
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Do people have trained capability to execute, detect abnormality, recover and improve—not just responsibility?
  - Is cross-skilling supported by time, pay and staffing rather than used as headcount extraction?
  - Is competence verified and refreshed when the baseline changes?

#### EL-022 — Safe voice, participation and bounded change authority

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-022
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Safe voice, participation and bounded change authority
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Suppressed alarms, learned helplessness, management blindness and pseudo-participation. Known over-application or implementation failures: Suggestion quotas; ignored andons; consultation without decision rights; retaliation; responsibility without resources; surveillance.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A governed rights-and-response system: safe signal, bounded local action, appeal, documented disposition and protection against workload transfer.
- **TRIGGER:** Front-line observation is necessary for quality, safety or improvement and management cannot centrally see conditions.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For simple low-risk work, a direct owner contact and documented response is enough.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Employment/psychological safety; response obligation; workload and time; representation; transparent decisions; escalation above local management.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MIXED_EMPIRICAL_HIGH_NORMATIVE; primary [S006], [S013], [S015]; critical [S025], [S026], [S029]; domain/empirical [S027], [S028], [S031]
- **CRITICISMS:** Toyota and Lean rhetoric often labels involvement 'empowerment' while pace, staffing and strategic decisions remain managerial.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Suggestion boxes, quality circles and daily huddles are optional; real voice and decision rights are the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-007, EL-012, EL-017, EL-023
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Can workers safely signal, stop, challenge and propose change, and must the organisation respond?
  - What decisions are worker-local, team, supervisory, cross-functional or system-owner?
  - Does participation change decisions or merely supply unpaid improvement labour?

#### EL-023 — Employment security, trust and fair distribution of productivity gains

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-023
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Employment security, trust and fair distribution of productivity gains
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Trust destruction, sabotage, hidden problems, fear-based compliance and 'lean and mean' layoffs. Known over-application or implementation failures: Promises withdrawn in downturns; gains captured by owners; redeployment becomes intensification; insiders protected while suppliers/contractors absorb risk.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Before labour-saving improvement, define employment, workload, compensation, redeployment and supplier effects; treat trust as evidence-bearing governance.
- **TRIGGER:** Improvement solicits worker knowledge, reduces labour content or changes job design materially.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For small changes with no employment/workload effect, state that boundary and monitor cumulative effects.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Credible management commitment; financial capacity; worker representation/voice; redeployment and training; transparent gain accounting.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MIXED_CONTEXTUAL; primary [S013], [S014], [S015]; critical [S025], [S029], [S070]; domain/empirical [S026], [S027], [S031]
- **CRITICISMS:** Toyota's 1950 dispute and wider Lean layoffs show security is not inherent; national institutions and firm economics matter.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Respect-for-people slogans, town halls and culture statements do not establish security or fair gains.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-006, EL-009, EL-015, EL-035
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Could surfacing or removing work threaten employment, pay, workload or supplier viability?
  - What credible protections and gain-sharing arrangements make participation rational?
  - Are labour harms measured alongside flow and cost outcomes?

#### EL-024 — Management responsibility for system conditions and escalation response

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-024
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Management responsibility for system conditions and escalation response
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Blame shifting, repeated local fixes, ignored escalation and transformation decay. Known over-application or implementation failures: Leader gemba theatre; delegation without resources; hero intervention; changing priorities; local blame; executive metrics conflict with doctrine.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Leaders own the conditions they control, respond to escalations, arbitrate cross-system trade-offs and are measured on outcomes, resilience and worker effects.
- **TRIGGER:** A problem exceeds local authority, recurs across teams or reflects structural operating conditions.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a bounded local issue, delegate action with a clear escalation threshold.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Authority over resources and policy; transparent response expectations; independent review; outcome and labour measures.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_CONVERGENT; primary [S005], [S010], [S013]; critical [S024], [S029], [S030]; domain/empirical [S027], [S030], [S044]
- **CRITICISMS:** Proponent accounts often make management commitment an unfalsifiable explanation for success or failure.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Leader standard work, gemba walks and coaching kata are optional; accountable system condition ownership is the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-022, EL-025, EL-038
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Are repeated local failures traceable to conditions only management or a system owner can change?
  - Does escalation produce a bounded response and decision?
  - Are leaders accountable for labour, resilience and incentive side effects, not only throughput?

#### EL-025 — Whole-system optimisation and proxy/metric governance

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-025
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Whole-system optimisation and proxy/metric governance
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Local optimisation, Goodhart effects, hidden queues, quality sacrifice and worker harm. Known over-application or implementation failures: Dashboard proliferation; composite-score opacity; denominator manipulation; item splitting; suppressed incidents; cross-context ranking; measurement burden.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Treat metrics as revisable evidence, not targets or truth; pair throughput with quality, outcome, resilience and human effects.
- **TRIGGER:** Metrics affect incentives or decisions across interacting components.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small team with direct outcome visibility, use a few narrative/quantitative checks rather than a dashboard programme.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Outcome model; stable metric definitions; cross-boundary data; qualitative challenge; no punishment for adverse signals.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT; primary [S005], [S018], [S053]; critical [S025], [S026], [S065]; domain/empirical [S058], [S059], [S062]
- **CRITICISMS:** Lean measures are frequently used for labour control; DORA and SPACE explicitly warn against single metrics and inappropriate comparisons.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Obeya boards, SQDC panels, burndowns or DORA dashboards are optional; decision-valid measurement is the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-001, EL-003, EL-009, EL-023
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Do metrics represent the whole outcome or create a local target that can be gamed?
  - Are quality, resilience, wellbeing and hidden queues countermeasured?
  - Can definitions and incentives be revised when behaviour adapts to the metric?

#### EL-026 — Equipment, service and delivery-system reliability and maintainability

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-026
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Equipment, service and delivery-system reliability and maintainability
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Unplanned downtime, repeated emergency repair, unsafe degradation and buffer depletion. Known over-application or implementation failures: Maintenance theatre; over-maintenance; bad predictive models; deferred maintenance to hit throughput; hidden technical debt; single-expert dependence.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Choose maintenance and recovery policy by criticality, failure distribution, consequence and replacement economics.
- **TRIGGER:** Failure interrupts flow materially, has safety/quality consequences or consumes strategic buffers.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For noncritical, cheap and rapidly replaceable components, use run-to-failure with spares and a tested replacement path.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Reliability reduces variability and buffer demand. Over-maintenance adds cost; under-maintenance causes disruption. Cheap path is tested run-to-failure for noncritical assets.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Criticality model; maintenance windows and capacity; skilled responders; spares; configuration history; feedback from incidents.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_CONVERGENT; primary [S005], [S004]; critical [S032], [S039]; domain/empirical [S041], [S042], [S057]
- **CRITICISMS:** Low inventory can convert ordinary failures into crises, while TPM programmes can become checklist bundles without reliability economics.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** TPM pillars, maintenance boards and autonomous-maintenance checklists are optional implementations.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-006, EL-008, EL-009
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the target identify reliability-critical equipment, services and dependencies?
  - Is maintenance chosen from failure and recovery economics rather than ritual frequency?
  - Would reducing buffers expose an unaddressed reliability defect?

#### EL-027 — Supplier and dependency capability development with fair risk allocation

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-027
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Supplier and dependency capability development with fair risk allocation
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Recurring supplier defects, unstable replenishment, adversarial cost transfer and weak joint learning. Known over-application or implementation failures: Coercive price reduction; unpaid improvement; lock-in; focal-firm appropriation of gains; supplier overinvestment; lower-tier labour harm.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Develop critical dependency capability only where joint value exceeds coordination and lock-in cost, with explicit fairness and resilience safeguards.
- **TRIGGER:** A dependency is recurrent, strategically material and improvable through relationship-specific learning.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For commodity, low-risk purchases, use clear specifications, diversified sources and routine qualification rather than a development programme.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Long-term relationship; technical openness; bargaining fairness; supplier capacity; protection of proprietary knowledge; exit alternatives.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_EMPIRICAL_MIXED_DISTRIBUTIONAL; primary [S005], [S010], [S069]; critical [S039], [S040]; domain/empirical [S038], [S041]
- **CRITICISMS:** Toyota partnership narratives can obscure power asymmetry; synchronisation may transfer buffers, capital and risk to suppliers.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Supplier kaizen workshops, scorecards or associations are optional; joint capability and fair allocation are the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-008, EL-023, EL-028, EL-039
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is a critical supplier being developed or merely pressured to absorb cost and inventory?
  - Are gains, buffers, workload and disruption risk allocated transparently and fairly?
  - Would diversification be cheaper and safer than deep relationship-specific development?

#### EL-028 — Multi-tier dependency visibility and resilience segmentation

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-028
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Multi-tier dependency visibility and resilience segmentation
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Correlated disruption, unknown single points of failure, slow recovery and false diversification. Known over-application or implementation failures: Huge stale maps; supplier confidentiality conflict; false sense of control; concentration persists despite visibility; mapping cost exceeds value.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Maintain decision-useful deep-tier visibility only for critical dependencies, coupled to buffer, alternate and recovery action.
- **TRIGGER:** A dependency can stop a material outcome, has long recovery, opaque tiers or geographic/technical concentration.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For low-criticality, rapidly substitutable inputs, record vendor/category and test replacement rather than map the whole network.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Resilience profile: visibility does not reduce flow by itself; it enables selective buffers, alternates and recovery. Overmapping wastes effort; undermapping hides correlated failure.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Supplier cooperation; data governance; item-site identity; criticality model; alternative qualification; regular updates.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** HIGH_POST_DISRUPTION_DOMAIN; primary [S035], [S036]; critical [S039], [S040]; domain/empirical [S037], [S040]
- **CRITICISMS:** Mapping does not itself create resilience and can shift reporting burden to small suppliers.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** A proprietary supply-chain map or Toyota RESCUE system is not required; a critical-dependency register may suffice.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-027, EL-034, EL-039
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Does the system know which lower-tier dependencies can stop the outcome and how long recovery takes?
  - Is visibility linked to an alternate, buffer, redesign or restoration decision?
  - Can low-criticality dependencies follow a cheap path instead of exhaustive mapping?

#### EL-029 — Set-based option preservation and delayed irreversible convergence

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-029
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Set-based option preservation and delayed irreversible convergence
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Premature convergence, expensive reversal and loss of feasible alternatives. Known over-application or implementation failures: Unbounded option proliferation; delayed decisions; duplicated work; superficial sets; hidden resource cost; no convergence rule.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Preserve only decision-relevant alternatives until evidence changes expected regret; record eliminated options and convergence rationale.
- **TRIGGER:** Uncertainty, coupling and irreversibility are high enough that a wrong early choice is costly and parallel learning is affordable.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** When the decision is reversible, familiar or low-coupling, choose one option and preserve a fallback note.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Exploration intentionally creates parallel WIP; favour it where option value exceeds queue and coordination cost. Conflicts with premature flow optimisation.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Capacity for parallel analysis; explicit decision criteria; integration/trade-off knowledge; convergence deadlines; ownership.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_CASE_BASED; primary [S049], [S050]; critical [S051], [S052]; domain/empirical [S049], [S052]
- **CRITICISMS:** Industrial implementation maturity and causal performance evidence remain limited; not every domain benefits.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Trade-off curves, set matrices and obeya rooms are optional; evidence-calibrated option preservation is the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-004, EL-005, EL-007, EL-036
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Is uncertainty and irreversibility high enough to justify preserving alternatives?
  - What evidence eliminates an option, and when must convergence occur?
  - Would parallel exploration reduce expected rework or merely duplicate effort?

#### EL-030 — Cross-functional product-development integration and reusable trade-off knowledge

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-030
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Cross-functional product-development integration and reusable trade-off knowledge
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Late engineering changes, handoff loss, manufacturing/service incompatibility and repeated analysis. Known over-application or implementation failures: Meeting overload; heavyweight bureaucracy; knowledge repositories without use; dominant function suppresses dissent; outdated trade-off curves.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Integrate affected disciplines at consequential decisions and preserve reusable evidence at interfaces—not every participant in every meeting.
- **TRIGGER:** Product outcomes couple multiple disciplines, lifecycle stages or downstream operations.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small single-discipline change, direct peer review and a concise decision record suffice.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Representative disciplines; decision authority; shared models; knowledge stewardship; incentives for lifecycle rather than functional optimisation.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_COMPARATIVE_AUTOMOTIVE; primary [S049], [S050]; critical [S051], [S052]; domain/empirical [S049], [S052]
- **CRITICISMS:** Automotive evidence may not transfer; LPD lacks agreed definition and complete coherent implementation evidence.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Obeya rooms, chief-engineer titles and trade-off curves are optional implementations.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-002, EL-020, EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Are coupled disciplines represented before irreversible decisions?
  - Is trade-off and constraint evidence reusable and freshness-controlled?
  - Would an integration mechanism resolve actual late conflict or only create more meetings?

#### EL-031 — Software delivery flow with automation, observability, reliability and recovery

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-031
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Software delivery flow with automation, observability, reliability and recovery
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Large risky releases, slow feedback, hidden operational defects and speed-at-the-expense-of-stability. Known over-application or implementation failures: Pipeline theatre; flaky tests; metric gaming; developer burnout; rapid delivery of low-value work; fragile platforms; operations burden hidden.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Optimise sustainable software change across throughput and instability, with small reversible changes, outcome validation and humane operations.
- **TRIGGER:** Software changes are frequent enough that manual release and delayed integration impose risk or delay.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For infrequent low-risk software, use reproducible builds, basic automated tests and a documented release/recovery path.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** Value: shorter software feedback and smaller failure scope. Assumes automation quality, operability and recovery. Overuse creates metric gaming, toil and acceleration without value.
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Version control; trustworthy tests; environment/configuration control; observability; on-call/recovery capability; product outcome feedback.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_LARGE_OBSERVATIONAL; primary [S053], [S054], [S057]; critical [S055], [S056], [S059]; domain/empirical [S055], [S056], [S058]
- **CRITICISMS:** Software evidence is largely observational and case-based; factory metaphors miss discovery, architecture and invisible cognitive work.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** A kanban board, daily deployment or DORA dashboard is not required; reliable, observable and reversible delivery capability is the property.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-001, EL-004, EL-023, EL-025
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Can the system deliver small changes safely, observe effects and recover without heroics?
  - Are delivery metrics used as system signals rather than individual targets?
  - Does faster delivery improve user/system outcomes or merely increase change volume?

#### EL-032 — Portfolio selection, stopping and retirement of low-value work

- **PROPERTY_ID:** EL-032
- **PROPERTY_NAME:** Portfolio selection, stopping and retirement of low-value work
- **FAILURE_MODE:** Portfolio congestion, zombie work, sunk-cost continuation and capacity starvation. Known over-application or implementation failures: Politically convenient cancellation; short-termism; abandoned users; hidden transition costs; gaming by redefining work as mandatory.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Stop or retire work only through an evidence-bearing decision that accounts for beneficiary, option value, transition and external costs.
- **TRIGGER:** Demand exceeds capacity, work ages without evidence, or a value hypothesis fails.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a tiny backlog, review informally at a defined interval and close stale items.
- **FLOW_RESILIENCE_PROFILE:** NOT_PRIMARY_FLOW_PROPERTY
- **REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS:** Outcome evidence; total-cost visibility; authority to stop; reversible archival/transition; stakeholder communication.
- **EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_CONVERGENT; primary [S018], [S053]; critical [S060], [S065]; domain/empirical [S044], [S058], [S062]
- **CRITICISMS:** Waste language can erase care, resilience or exploratory work whose value is indirect or delayed.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Backlog purges, stop-start-continue workshops and portfolio kanban are optional.
- **POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY:** EL-001, EL-008, EL-029
- **QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT:**
  - Can low-value or stale work be stopped, deferred and retired rather than merely completed faster?
  - Does the stop decision account for transition, users, resilience and option value?
  - Is a lightweight periodic review sufficient for this portfolio?

### CEREMONIES_TO_NOT_BLINDLY_ADOPT

| PRACTICE | UNDERLYING_PROPERTY | REQUIRED? | ANTI_BLIND_ADOPTION_RULE |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Andon cord/button/light | EL-011 abnormality detection/signalling plus EL-012 bounded escalation/containment. | NO | Retain action-linked signalling and response tiers; do not copy a cord or assume signal equals stop. |
| Kanban card/bin | EL-004 explicit WIP/release constraint plus EL-010 replenishment information integrity. | NO | Retain explicit release/WIP logic; the physical card is one historical implementation. |
| Heijunka box / levelling board | EL-007 demand-capacity pacing and levelling. | NO | Use only where aggregation and repetitive pacing are economically valid. |
| Takt-time display | EL-007 explicit demand-capacity pacing and EL-003 state legibility. | NO | Manufacturing-specific in its classic form; do not translate every knowledge task into a takt clock. |
| One-piece flow / manufacturing cell | EL-005 batch economics, EL-006 setup reduction and EL-002 end-to-end flow. | NO | Treat batch size as an optimisation variable; reject smallest-possible-batch dogma. |
| Value-stream map | EL-002 end-to-end queue/handoff visibility and EL-025 whole-system optimisation. | NO | Use the cheapest current model that changes a decision; wall-sized notation has no intrinsic value. |
| 5S programme/audit | EL-003 workplace/work-state legibility and selected safety/search controls. | NO | Retain legibility where search/ambiguity is costly; reject ritual scores and universal 5S campaigns. |
| Shadow board / floor marking | EL-003 point-of-work state legibility. | NO | Useful only when absence/location causes a material failure mode. |
| Standard-work sheet | EL-017 explicit current baseline with controlled revision. | NO | Require baseline identity, scope, exceptions, owner, revision authority and review/retirement—not a Toyota form. |
| A3 report | EL-019 evidence-bound problem framing, causal inquiry, countermeasure and qualification. | NO | Retain concise traceable reasoning only where it improves a decision; exact paper size/boxes are ceremony. |
| Five Whys | EL-019 causal depth beyond the immediate symptom. | NO | Use only as an initial prompt for simple, well-bounded problems; never treat five answers as causal proof. |
| PDCA/PDSA cycle | EL-019 iterative prediction-bearing tests and EL-020 sustainment. | NO | Retain disciplined iteration; cycle names and diagrams have no evidentiary force. |
| Kaizen event / rapid-improvement event | EL-019 and EL-020 focused local improvement and sustainment. | NO | Trigger only for a bounded problem with authority, baseline, follow-up and sustainment economics. |
| Gemba walk / genchi genbutsu visit | EL-018 direct observation combined with traceable evidence. | NO | Retain direct evidence acquisition but triangulate it; presence at a location is not causal understanding. |
| Poka-yoke device/check fixture | EL-014 mistake-proofing and fail-safe constraints. | NO | Use when the error mode and safe constraint are understood and an escape/review path exists. |
| Quality circle / suggestion scheme | EL-021 capability and EL-022 safe participation/change authority. | NO | Retain real voice, resources and feedback; participation theatre is not respect. |
| Obeya / visual war room | EL-002 integration, EL-003 shared state and EL-030 cross-functional product-development coordination. | NO | Use a shared decision environment only when coordination latency is material. |
| Autonomation/jidoka equipment | EL-015 automate routine operation while reserving human exception judgement, plus EL-011 detection. | NO | Automate only with bounded authority, observable state, safe fallback and competent human response. |
| SMED workshop / setup chart | EL-006 setup/changeover latency reduction. | NO | Retain setup-cost reduction where it changes batch or recovery economics; reject workshop ritual. |
| Daily management board / tiered huddle | EL-003 current state, EL-011 abnormalities, EL-024 response ownership and EL-025 metric governance. | NO | Use only when cadence and display shorten response; routine status recitation is ceremony. |
| Supplier kanban / tightly synchronised deliveries | EL-010 information integrity, EL-027 supplier capability and EL-028 dependency resilience. | NO | Never universalise; choose per dependency criticality, turbulence, lead time and recovery option. |
| Software kanban board | EL-004 WIP/release control, EL-002 flow visibility and EL-003 state legibility. | NO | Board optional; retain WIP/queue decisions only when item identity and workflow state are meaningful. |
| DORA or cycle-time dashboard | EL-025 proxy governance and EL-031 software delivery flow/reliability. | NO | Use as diagnostic signals with guardrails, never as individual productivity quotas or proof of value. |
| Set-based matrix / trade-off curve | EL-029 option preservation and EL-030 reusable trade-off knowledge. | NO | Use only under material uncertainty and irreversible coupling; exact Toyota artefacts are not required. |

### CONTEXTS_WHERE_PROPERTY_SHOULD_NOT_TRIGGER

| CONTEXT | DO_NOT_TRIGGER | CHEAP_PATH | PROPERTIES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Trivial, low-risk, reversible work with one owner and obvious state | Value-stream mapping, formal A3, tiered boards, elaborate standard-work lifecycle | One-sentence purpose, acceptance condition and direct execution. | EL-001; EL-002; EL-017; EL-019 |
| No meaningful recurring queue or commitment overload | Kanban board or WIP-limit programme | Observe actual lead time and only add an admission rule if congestion emerges. | EL-004 |
| High setup/validation cost dominates feedback value | Smallest possible batch or continuous release | Reduce one setup cost or select an economic transfer batch. | EL-005; EL-006; EL-036 |
| Novel exploratory work with no stable demand unit | Takt clock, heijunka or forced linear flow | Use milestone/option evidence, workload envelope and review cadence. | EL-007; EL-029 |
| Stable process with negligible consequence and cheap recovery | High-cost redundancy or full resilience programme | Name the current time/capacity fallback and review after material change. | EL-008; EL-028 |
| Detector is noisy and propagation cost is low | Automatic hard stop on every signal | Log/triage first, tune threshold, then add scoped containment. | EL-011; EL-012 |
| Error mode is poorly understood or legitimate variation is wide | Hard poka-yoke/interlock | Improve observation and classify cases before constraining them. | EL-014; EL-016 |
| Work changes faster than a baseline can be maintained | Detailed immutable procedure | Record constraints, examples and decision principles with an expiry date. | EL-017; EL-040 |
| One-off, non-recurrent loss smaller than investigation cost | Full RCA, A3 or kaizen event | Contain, record uncertainty and set a recurrence trigger. | EL-019; EL-020 |
| Team lacks protected time, authority or psychological safety | Suggestion campaign or continuous-improvement quota | First provide response capacity, decision rights and employment/workload safeguards. | EL-021; EL-022; EL-023; EL-038 |
| Supplier is commodity, low criticality and easily replaceable | Deep supplier-development network or full multi-tier mapping | Basic quality/service agreement and monitor concentration. | EL-027; EL-028 |
| Choice is cheap, reversible and evidence is quickly available | Large set-based option portfolio | Run the smallest discriminating experiment and decide. | EL-029 |
| Software release is rare for regulatory or physical-coupling reasons | Frequency maximisation | Automate qualification/rehearsal and improve rollback/recovery without inventing releases. | EL-031 |
| Work has mandatory maintenance, safety, legal or option value | Stop/retire merely because near-term customer value is low | Classify obligation/option/resilience value and review at the proper horizon. | EL-032 |
| Existing native mechanism already closes the failure mode | Any named Lean practice or duplicate governance artefact | Document equivalence and test the native mechanism. | EL-037 |

### MANUFACTURING_SPECIFIC_PROPERTIES

| PROPERTY_OR_FORM | PROPERTY_IDS | LIMIT | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Classic takt time and heijunka sequence | EL-007 | Requires recurring demand units, measurable work content and controllable mix; use workload/capacity approaches elsewhere. | [S004], [S063], [S064] |
| Physical kanban containers/cards between repeat processes | EL-004, EL-010 | The general property is WIP/replenishment control; physical signalling is domain-specific. | [S003], [S006], [S032] |
| One-piece moving assembly flow and manufacturing cells | EL-005, EL-006 | Generalises only as batch/transfer economics and feedback; not every service or design path is linear. | [S004], [S032] |
| Fixed-position assembly-line stop with small line buffers | EL-012 | Generalises to bounded containment/escalation; exact geometry/timing is manufacturing-specific. | [S004], [S005] |
| Standard WIP as part of standardised assembly work | EL-017 | Generalises to explicit baseline; classic takt/sequence/standard-WIP form does not fit all work. | [S004] |
| SMED stamping-die changeover practice | EL-006 | Generalises to setup/release latency reduction, not the specific industrial technique. | [S004], [S007] |
| Supplier milk-runs and tightly timed automotive replenishment | EL-010, EL-027, EL-028 | Depends on geography, lead time, volume, transport and risk; no universal supply policy. | [S005], [S040], [S069] |
| Machine autonomation from textile/automotive equipment | EL-015 | Generalises to bounded automation and human exception handling; detector/stop implementation is technology-specific. | [S001], [S067] |
| Physical 5S/shadow-board/floor-layout controls | EL-003 | Generalises only where physical search, missing tools or ergonomic state are material. | [S066] |
| Automotive set-based concurrent engineering implementation | EL-029, EL-030 | The option and trade-off properties may transfer; exact organisation and artefacts are domain-specific. | [S049], [S050], [S052] |

### SOCIO_TECHNICAL_PRECONDITIONS

| PRECONDITION | WHY_REQUIRED | EVIDENCE_OR_TEST | SOURCES |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Non-retaliatory abnormality signalling | EL-011/EL-012 fail when workers hide problems to avoid blame or output penalties. | Signal use, response and retaliation evidence; anonymous/worker testimony; production-pressure exceptions. | [S025], [S029], [S031] |
| Real decision rights matched to accountability | Participation without authority becomes pseudo-empowerment or extra labour. | Authority matrix, examples of accepted/rejected worker changes, escalation outcomes. | [S006], [S026], [S027] |
| Protected response and improvement capacity | Signals, kaizen and problem solving overload workers/supervisors when no capacity exists. | Response latency, staffing, improvement time, backlog and overtime. | [S032], [S043], [S070] |
| Training and capability, not attendance | Baseline, causal analysis and local change require technical and relational skill. | Observed competence, coaching quality, cross-skill coverage and safe exception handling. | [S008], [S009], [S021] |
| Employment security or credible transition/fair-gain arrangement | Workers rationally resist exposing labour-saving improvements when gains threaten their livelihood. | Layoff/redeployment policy, gain sharing, contingent-worker treatment and downturn record. | [S013], [S014], [S023], [S031] |
| Manageable workload and ergonomic design | Lean can intensify pace and reduce autonomy, especially in low-complexity manual work. | Work intensity, recovery time, injuries, autonomy, skill use and well-being—not only output. | [S025], [S026], [S028], [S070] |
| Management ownership of structural causes | Local teams cannot kaizen away staffing, architecture, incentives or supplier policy controlled elsewhere. | Escalated causes receive resources/decisions; recurring local workarounds decline. | [S020], [S024], [S043] |
| Fair supplier/customer risk allocation | Low inventory and synchronisation can externalise buffers and labour pressure. | Where inventory/capacity cost sits, payment terms, forecast changes, disruption burden and supplier voice. | [S035], [S039], [S040] |
| Stable definitions and non-punitive metrics | Visual control and flow metrics become surveillance or gaming when used as individual quotas. | Metric purpose, access, definition changes, gaming audits and decision examples. | [S025], [S058], [S059] |
| Mechanism-linked outcome and adverse-effect evidence | Local process gains can coexist with worse worker, patient, supplier or resilience outcomes. | Outcome chain, countermetrics, sustainment horizon and transferred-cost analysis. | [S060], [S061], [S062] |

| EL-004 | Conditional pull/release control and explicit WIP limits | HIGH_MECHANISTIC_MODERATE_EMPIRICAL | [S003], [S005], [S006], [S032], [S034], [S055] | What causal evidence is sufficient to distinguish a useful limit from demand suppression or work reclassification? |
| EL-006 | Setup, changeover, release and feedback latency reduction | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S004], [S007], [S032], [S057] | Which transition costs are shifted to suppliers, operations or workers and therefore missed by local measures? |
| EL-008 | Explicit buffer portfolio and strategic slack | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S002], [S006], [S032], [S034], [S040] | How should correlated and deep-tier risks be priced when probabilities are poorly known? |
| EL-009 | Flow and queue-aware capacity over maximum local utilisation | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S003], [S005], [S032], [S056], [S062] | How should public or safety-critical systems value reserve capacity whose benefit appears only during peaks? |
| EL-011 | Abnormality detection and signalling | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S001], [S004], [S005], [S067], [S031], [S067] | How should detection thresholds be tuned when misses are catastrophic but false alarms erode response capacity? |
| EL-012 | Bounded stop, containment, escalation and recovery authority | HIGH_HISTORICAL_MECHANISTIC | [S004], [S005], [S006], [S067], [S031] | Direct comparative evidence on stop-authority effectiveness under real production pressure and false alarms remains limited. |
| EL-013 | Quality at source and defect containment | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S001], [S005], [S010], [S041], [S057], [S061] | How should source checks be balanced against independent assurance for systemic or adversarial failures? |
| EL-016 | Process capability, variation measurement and statistical quality control | HIGH_HISTORICAL_EMPIRICAL | [S010], [S011], [S041], [S042], [S062] | How should sparse, nonstationary and dependent observations be governed without false statistical certainty? |
| EL-019 | Evidence-bound problem solving and countermeasure qualification | HIGH_CONVERGENT_WEAK_FORMAT_SPECIFIC | [S007], [S010], [S012], [S043], [S047] | Which problem classes justify experimentation, formal causal analysis or independent review remains context-dependent. |
| EL-024 | Management responsibility for system conditions and escalation response | MODERATE_CONVERGENT | [S005], [S010], [S013], [S027], [S030], [S044] | How can management responsibility be measured without turning 'leadership commitment' into a post hoc success explanation? |
| EL-026 | Equipment, service and delivery-system reliability and maintainability | HIGH_CONVERGENT | [S005], [S004], [S041], [S042], [S057] | How should maintenance policy incorporate correlated software, cyber and supplier failures with sparse history? |
| EL-028 | Multi-tier dependency visibility and resilience segmentation | HIGH_POST_DISRUPTION_DOMAIN | [S035], [S036], [S037], [S040] | Methods for validating supplier-reported lower-tier data and common-cause dependence remain immature. |

### PROPOSITIONS_WITH_STRONG_CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

| PROPERTY_ID | PROPOSITION | CURRENT_STATUS | CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH | REPLACEMENT |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EL-033 | Universal waste elimination as the master objective | SUPERSEDED_BY_STRONGER_FORM | HIGH_CRITICAL_CONVERGENT | No independent property. Ask which failure mode the alleged waste causes and what protective function it may serve. |
| EL-034 | Minimum or zero inventory everywhere | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | HIGH_CONTRARY_EVIDENCE | NO GENERAL PROPERTY; choose inventory by total expected cost and resilience. |
| EL-035 | Maximum utilisation and zero slack | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | HIGH_CONTRARY_EVIDENCE | Target sustainable service and total cost, not maximum busy time. |
| EL-036 | One-piece flow or the smallest possible batch everywhere | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | HIGH_MECHANISTIC_CONTEXTUAL | NO GENERAL PROPERTY; choose reversible economic batches by context. |
| EL-037 | Exact Toyota-tool or named-method conformance | CEREMONY_NOT_GENERAL_PROPERTY | HIGH_CONVERGENT | NO GENERAL PROPERTY. Evaluate mechanism, trigger, burden, alternatives and outcome. |
| EL-038 | Improvement activity volume as evidence of improvement | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | HIGH_CRITICAL | Count evidence-qualified outcomes and retired failures, not ritual volume. |
| EL-039 | Universal supplier synchronisation, concentration or single sourcing | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | HIGH_POST_DISRUPTION | Choose relationship depth and source redundancy by criticality, learning value and recovery economics. |
| EL-040 | Immutable standardisation and procedure compliance | REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED | HIGH_MIXED_SOCIAL | No immutable general standard; use risk-calibrated constraints within a versioned and reviewable baseline. |

### UNRESOLVED_PROPERTIES

No entire denominator property is left **unexamined**. No final property uses `CURRENT_STATUS = UNRESOLVED`; where the evidence does not support universal retention, the property is marked `CONTEXT_DEPENDENT`, `SOCIO_TECHNICAL_CONTEXT_PROPERTY`, `USEFUL_BUT_EASILY_GAMED`, mixed-strength, rejected or superseded. The 24 explicit unresolved or contested research questions remain in `EVOLVED_LEAN_OPEN_QUESTIONS` and must not be silently converted into adoption claims.

### Frozen repository-audit posture

The later repository auditor should ask, for every property:

1. Is the underlying failure mode actually present?
2. Does the target already close it through a native mechanism?
3. Are the trigger and socio-technical/technical preconditions satisfied?
4. Is there a cheap non-trigger path?
5. Would the candidate mechanism improve an end-to-end outcome, or only a local proxy?
6. What buffer, resilience, autonomy, supplier or learning capacity might it remove?
7. What evidence would qualify the countermeasure and what would retire it?
8. Is the proposal importing a useful property, or only Toyota/Lean terminology and ceremony?

Those questions are intentionally **not answered** in this external research lane.
